PR 4596 review: attention path stats behind TRON_ATTN_STATS

2026-09-30 · reviewer: Claude, following the tron-code-review skill (the review checklist for tron pull requests) · reviewed head 04da001cb5 (PR head now 0f784c44ff after the follow-up commits) on branch jhan-attn-path-stats, diff base 66c7bb8db1 (the main commit the branch started from) · verdict approve with nits

Short version. TRON_ATTN_STATS is a new environment variable of tron (the inference program). Set to "1", it makes PR #4596 count how much attention work ran on each CPU or FPGA path and how long attention took. The numbers are published as FUSE leaves (files under /model/<id>/attention/) and as a stderr summary. Verdict: approve with nits. No finding shows a wrong number, a crash or a race in production. 7 should-fix items remain: - 4 CI (continuous integration, the automatic test run on each push) test gaps at production hook call sites - 1 test that asserts an implementation detail - 1 PR body whose performance and instruction-count claims were measured ten commits before HEAD - 1 missing sample count for the T3 (worker-0 job wall) timer / The head's four unit-test binaries were built and run on this host (claude-box, no AMX unit, no FPGA) with the switch unset, set to 1 and set to yes. All 12 runs (4 binaries x 3 settings) passed. The author's posted runtron numbers are consistent with the code at HEAD.

After the review, five of the seven should-fix items were closed by seven test commits pushed on 2026-09-30 (PR head now 0f784c44ff). The two open items are the stale PR-body performance claims (finding R1-M13 in the Findings section) and the missing T3 sample count (finding R1-M27).

Words used here

  • tron: the inference program under test. runtron is its command-line tool. rinzler is the production server that runs tron.
  • PR 4596: the pull request under review (positron-ai/tron, branch jhan-attn-path-stats). Head = the last commit of the branch, 04da001cb5. Base = the main commit the branch started from, 66c7bb8db1.
  • TRON_ATTN_STATS: the environment variable of this PR. Exactly the value 1 turns the attention path statistics on at run time. Any other value is ignored with a warning.
  • AVX, AMX, FPGA: the three paths that can score attention. AVX and AMX are CPU instruction sets (AMX is the newer matrix unit). The FPGA is a programmable accelerator card. FPGA-assisted attention can still include software work.
  • TRON_AMX_DISABLE: the kill switch: with it set, an AMX-capable binary takes the AVX path.
  • token job: one token's scheduled model work. A forward is one model execution over all scheduled token jobs. A listener receives a token job's output scores when they are ready. A job without a listener only stores its K and V (key and value) vectors (kv_only).
  • forward class: decode_like when every token job of the forward has a listener, prompt_or_mixed when at least one job lacks one. Every counter of this PR is kept per class.
  • visit: one call of apply_page_tok: one (query token, KV page of 64 tokens, KV head) triple. The AMX kernel serves a whole dense page (a page whose 64 tokens all lie inside one visible range of the query) per visit. The AVX loop serves only the relevant K tokens of the page. An empty visit found no software work.
  • ready page / pending page: a KV page is ready when it was complete before the forward started, and pending when the current forward is still filling it.
  • KV page, layer, KV head, kv_mul: a KV page is a 64-token block of the key/value cache. A layer is one transformer block. A KV head is one key/value attention channel. kv_mul is the number of query heads per KV head (grouped-query attention).
  • pool worker / attention worker: a pool worker is one thread of the application thread pool, indexed from 0. The attention workers of a forward are the last n_attn_workers pool workers. Since the round-4 commit bff317e0d3, every per-worker row is keyed by pool worker index.
  • T1 to T5: the five timers of the PR, all in TSC cycles (the CPU time-stamp counter, converted with tsc_hz from the summary leaf). T1 = wall time of one forward. T2 = busy time of one attention job on one worker. T3 = wall time of one attention job as attention worker 0 sees it. T4 = layer period on attention worker 0. T5 = join_wait_cycles, the time a worker waits in the join with no progress.
  • join: the step where one worker combines the partial attention results of the other workers (software join) or waits for an FPGA pass (hardware join).
  • FUSE leaf: a virtual file under /model/<id>/attention/ that renders one JSON (JavaScript Object Notation) object when read. The leaves are summary, <class>_totals, <class>_forwards, <class>_layer_<L> and <class>_worker_<W>.
  • forward_scope: the object of attn_stats.hpp that owns the per-forward record: constructed at the start of model::state::forward, it stores the class and the T1 start, and its destructor files T1, the job counts and the per-token path sets.
  • hook: a method of model_stats (the stats object of attn_stats.hpp) that production code calls: note_attn_job, add_visits, note_join_wait, note_fpga_pass, note_fpga_query, note_attn_workers and the like.
  • TRON_ASSERT: the repository's assertion macro, active in every build. A failed assertion aborts the process.
  • W1 to W5: the five review comments Wade posted on 2026-09-28. Each has a round-4 fix commit on the branch.
  • EAGLE: a speculative-decoding scheme with a small draft model. The draft state shares the target model's id.
  • severity words: must fix = wrong numbers, a crash or a data race in production. should fix = wrong under a reachable but uncommon condition, or a test that cannot fail. nit = wording or style. info = a design fact worth knowing.
  • evidence strength: confirmed by code = the reviewer quoted the lines at the head that show it. plausible = the reading is consistent but one step was not traced. hypothesis = a suspected condition whose reachability was not shown.
  • discriminating check: a test or measurement whose outcome differs between correct behavior and the suspected defect.

Scope, method, what was actually run

This page reviews pull request 4596 of positron-ai/tron at its head commit 04da001cb5 (pushed 2026-09-29 23:57 UTC). The diff base is 66c7bb8db1, the main commit the branch started from. The diff touches 11 files and adds 2,501 lines: the new header h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp (953 lines), hooks in h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp and h/tron/models/model.hpp, README.stats.md, and six test files including the new t/t_attn_stats.cpp (932 lines). The main branch has moved 161 commits past the base and touches model.hpp and self_attention.hpp. The main-drift lens below checked whether the PR still applies (a lens is one reading pass over the diff with one question in mind).

The review followed the tron-code-review skill (the review checklist this reviewer uses for tron pull requests). The steps applied were:

  • trace every execution path the change crosses (forward bookkeeping, visit tallies, timers, the FPGA path, asynchronous ownership, worker and layer indexing)
  • check each counter's semantics and owner
  • check the relevant conditions
  • review whether the tests can fail Nineteen reading lenses produced 128 raw findings. Duplicates were merged. Three independent refuters (checks that try to disprove a finding, here on reachability, on evidence at the head and on intent) checked each merged finding, and a finding was dropped when two of the three refuted it. A completeness critic (a separate pass that looks for review questions the first round missed) chose the seven second-round lenses. The author's 2026-09-30 test-data comments were checked separately (section 'Posted test data').

Verdict

No must_fix defect survived the 19 review lenses and the 3 refuters per finding. No wrong number in production, no crash and no race was confirmed at HEAD 04da001cb5 (findings-compact.json: 0 must_fix, 7 should_fix, 76 nit, 20 info). The 7 should_fix items are listed below (R1-M1 and the like are the finding ids of the first review round):

  • 4 test gaps: production hook call sites that no test in CI (the repository's automated test runs) drives (R1-M1, R1-M2, R1-M8, R1-M9)
  • 1 test that asserts on an implementation detail, against the repo test policy (R1-M15)
  • 1 stale PR-body claim: the counts from perf (the Linux profiler) and from objdump (the tool that lists a binary's machine instructions) were measured at cbf1bb6c0c, not at HEAD (R1-M13)
  • 1 missing sample count for the T3 timer (R1-M27) The head's four unit-test binaries were built and run on this host with TRON_ATTN_STATS unset, "1" and "yes". All 12 runs passed (buildtest.json).

The author's 2026-09-30 test-data comments are arithmetically consistent with HEAD (testdata-result.json). Their one correction is the wording of where the numbers came from. The posted run is of bff317e0d3, one commit before HEAD, with the same end-of-run values.

Tests actually run for this review

The PR head 04da001cb5 was configured and built on claude-box (an AMD Zen 5 host with 32 cores and no AMX unit) inside the repository's nix shell (nix is the package manager that supplies the build tools). The build settings were: the native preset (the repository's default local build configuration, which probes the host CPU's support for AVX512, the 512-bit-wide AVX extension), RelWithDebInfo (an optimized build that keeps debug symbols), TRON_AMX_DISPATCH=ON (the build option that compiles the AMX kernels in) and no model exports (BUILD_INGEST_MODELS=OFF). The configure took 24 s and the build of the four test targets took 370 s (with hits from ccache, the compiler output cache). The four binaries were then run from the worktree root (the git checkout directory of the branch) three times each: with TRON_ATTN_STATS unset, set to 1, and set to yes. All 12 runs passed.

This host has no AMX unit and no FPGA. So the AMX kernel path and the FPGA path ran only through the test fakes (stand-in objects that the tests supply in place of the hardware). The runtron measurements of the PR body and of the 2026-09-30 comments come from the author's runs on delphi-3bda (the Intel test server with AMX and FPGA cards), not from this review.

  • All 19 test cases of t/t_attn_stats.cpp are listed by t_llama_unit --list-tests and ran. The binary reports 62 cases. The PR body's count of 55 cases and 219,937 assertions was taken at d85edc0e28, before the round-4 tests.
  • The 'ignored' warning for TRON_ATTN_STATS=yes prints once per process, from the once-per-process static in env_enabled() [h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:161-172]. t_llama_unit printed it 7 times. 6 lines came from inside the test case 'dynamic attention descriptors match the active operation'. That case runs each probe in a forked child process [t/assertion_signal.hpp, calls at t/t_llama_unit.cpp:1458-1487]. Every child initializes its own copy of the static. The seventh line came from the parent process in the next case. That is expected behavior, not a defect.
  • Every binary prints two 'hwloc_set_cpubind for thread failed' error lines at start. They come from the CPU binding of the container (claude-box runs as a Linux container). They are present with and without the PR. They are unrelated to the PR.
  • The 4 warnings in the build log are nix 'trusted-public-keys' setting warnings. None comes from a compiler.
  • Logs are in the scratchpad directory buildtest/ (configure.log, build.log, one .out file per binary and switch value, summary.tsv). The runner is buildtest/run-tests.sh.

Code inspection

Every finding below cites line numbers of the head commit 04da001cb5. They shift after the next push. Line numbers of the earlier heads (1430736b2a, the one Wade reviewed) are not used. Each finding states its evidence strength. 'Confirmed by code' means the reviewer quoted the lines that show it. 'Plausible' means one step of the reading was not traced. 'Hypothesis' means the condition's reachability was not shown.

Findings are grouped into nine themes and, inside each theme, ordered by severity (should_fix, then nit, then info). Severity means here: should_fix = wrong under a reachable but uncommon condition, or a test that cannot fail on a real break, or a claim in the PR text that is not true of HEAD. nit = wording, style or a one-line hardening. info = a design fact written down for the reader, with no change asked. The three rejected findings (R1-M23, R1-M51, R2-M14) are listed in report-base.json with their rejection reasons. They are not repeated here.

Read these first

  1. R1-M1: No test runs a real model forward with stats on. The class rule and job count therefore go unchecked
  2. R1-M2: No test reads the per-forward path-set histogram after a real apply_page_range run
  3. R1-M8: The T2, T3 and T4 hook calls inside run_attention_job run in no test with stats on
  4. R1-M27: T3 (wall_cycles_w0) has no sample count. The mean job wall time therefore cannot be derived from the leaves
  5. R1-M13: Pull request performance claims (timing table, instruction counts) predate HEAD by ten commits
  6. R1-M9: The FPGA pass and FPGA query hook call sites are executed by no unit test
  7. R1-M15: Test case 'the hooks alone reproduce the half record' asserts on an implementation detail
  8. R1-M7: T4 periods are keyed by operation change. Two attention ops on one layer would distort the counts
  9. R2-M13: Two live states with one model id share one leaf prefix. The first state's leaves are no longer visible
  10. R1-M38: n_attn_workers_min can read as UINT64_MAX (the largest 64-bit unsigned value) once if two relaxed stores (atomic writes with no ordering guarantee between them) are reordered
  11. R1-M75: The stderr report embeds the model id unescaped. An id with a quote, backslash or control character then yields invalid JSON
  12. R1-M3: A live read during the forward's end can show forwards one ahead of token_jobs

Findings

should fix (7)

R1-M27 T3 (wall_cycles_w0) has no sample count. The mean job wall time therefore cannot be derived from the leaves should fix design choice

Trigger

A run where n_attn_workers changes between forwards (rinzler: recommend_n_main_helpers picks the split (the number of attention workers) per forward, attn_stats.hpp:297-299) or where forwards carry several minibatches (a minibatch is one chunk of the forward's token jobs, scored as its own set of attention jobs) (llama plugin, the tron model code for llama-family models, llama.hpp:751-773). A reader wants the mean job wall on worker 0 for a layer.

Consequence

layer_timers carries wall_cycles and wall_max_cycles only (attn_stats.hpp:255-256). The layer leaf's attn_jobs is the sum over all workers (attn_stats.hpp:612, 651-658). So wall_cycles_w0 / attn_jobs is too small by the split. The split is only known as a min/max range (n_attn_workers_min/max). T4 has period_count (attn_stats.hpp:261). T3 has no companion count. In the PR-body sample the reader could divide by 183600 attention jobs / 20 workers. That worked only because the split was constant.

Where

h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:252, h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:515-520, h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:617-624, h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:686-691, README.stats.md:281-284

Evidence
252: struct alignas(64) layer_timers {
255:   std::atomic<uint64_t> wall_cycles{0};
256:   std::atomic<uint64_t> wall_max_cycles{0};
260:   std::atomic<uint64_t> period_cycles{0};
261:   std::atomic<uint64_t> period_count{0};
515: inline void note_attn_job_wall_w0(size_t layer, uint64_t wall_cycles) noexcept {
518:   inc_relaxed(t.wall_cycles, wall_cycles);
519:   max_relaxed(t.wall_max_cycles, wall_cycles);
686-691: "wall_cycles_w0":{},"wall_max_cycles_w0":{},"period_cycles_w0":{},"period_count_w0":{},"period_max_cycles_w0":{}
Evidence strength

confirmed by code

Discriminating check

Run rinzler with TRON_ATTN_STATS=1 under a load whose user count changes (so n_attn_workers_min != n_attn_workers_max in <class>_forwards). Try to compute the mean T3 per job for one layer from the leaves alone: no combination of wall_cycles_w0, attn_jobs, forwards, period_count gives the number of worker-0 jobs in that layer.

Suggested fix

Add std::atomic<uint64_t> wall_count{0} to layer_timers, increment it in note_attn_job_wall_w0, sum it in layer_sums::add_timers/add_sums, and render "wall_count_w0" next to wall_cycles_w0 (README.stats.md line 281-284 sentence gets the new key). One test line in the T2..T5 case (t_attn_stats.cpp:257-265) checks wall_count == 1 after one note_attn_job_wall_w0.

Verifier notes

reach: Holds: T3 has no sample count. The only derivation (layer attn_jobs / n_attn_workers) breaks when the per-forward split varies (rinzler estimator branch, model.hpp:2003-2030, note_attn_workers at model.hpp:2303). period_count_w0 is no substitute. It counts operations, not minibatch jobs (attn_stats.hpp:356-358), and it is 0 for the last layer. Correction: several minibatches with a constant split do NOT block the derivation on their own. The varying split does.

evidence: Holds, with a sharper trigger: a varying split alone does not block the mean. With one minibatch per forward, wall_cycles_w0 / forwards is exact. The PR-body sample is such a case: 255 forwards x 36 layers x 20 workers = 183600 attention jobs over all layers and workers. The blocker is the per-forward minibatch count (chunk_evenly, model.hpp:240-270, >= 2 chunks whenever a forward has more than 8 token jobs). No leaf records that count. period_count_w0 cannot substitute. It counts operations (one per layer per forward, attn_stats.hpp:354-358, 490) and skips the last layer. The finding's llama.hpp:751-773 citation does not exist at HEAD.

intent: Holds, with one softening: the T3 sample count summed over layers is recoverable from <class>_worker_<n_workers-1>.attn_jobs (the last pool worker is an attention worker in every forward, attn_stats.hpp:431-435). That derivation needs the row mapping and gives no per-layer value. Neither README.stats.md nor the Note comment in the code documents it. period_count_w0 is not a substitute. It counts once per operation, not per minibatch job (attn_stats.hpp:357-358, 485-500). chunk_evenly gives a multi-user decode forward 2 minibatches (model.hpp:245-249). So even the PR body's "20 attention workers = 183600/255/36" mixes the split and the minibatch count into one number, unless n_attn_workers_max confirmed 20.

R1-M9 The FPGA pass and FPGA query hook call sites are executed by no unit test should fix test gap

Trigger

Any FPGA-assisted run: prepare_uniform_hw_attention runs with a non-empty hw_plan (the query batch's list of FPGA passes) and uses_hw, and TRON_ATTN_STATS=1.

Consequence

A wrong relative index (e.g. an off-by-one in relative_hw_tok_ix's two return paths, or a loop bound other than B) would go unnoticed. The only tests of note_fpga_pass pass literal numbers to the hook (t/t_attn_stats.cpp:282-295, 869, 882).

R1-M43 Stats branches in the visit loop carry no unlikely hint; code layout is left to the compiler nit design choiceh/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1650
Trigger

Any build. The visit loop of apply_page_range (384 instantiations, +47,276 bytes total per the PR body) contains the on-state block if (stats_enabled) { stats_tally.record(...); path_bits[...] |= ...; } at 1650-1653 and the per-call block at 1680-1685. Neither block carries a branch-probability hint.

Consequence

With the switch off, the hot loop executes one bool test per visit (as designed). But the compiler may lay the on-state code inline in the fall-through path (the code that follows the branch when it is not taken). That placement costs instruction-cache (icache) and fetch bandwidth in the most frequently executed loop of the program. The PR's own A/A shows code-placement effects of the same order as the resolution (+0.77 % TPS at prompt 8192 tokens, attributed to placement). model.hpp already uses if tron_unlikely (cfg.log_intermediates) for a directly analogous runtime switch.

Where

h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1650, h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1680, h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:854, h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1275, h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1341

Evidence
1650: `          if (stats_enabled) {`
1651: `            stats_tally.record(visit.served, relevant_k_tokens, page::page_size);`
1652: `            path_bits[tok_ix.i] |= attn_stats::path_bit(visit.served);`
1680: `      if (stats_enabled) {`
h/common/attributes.hpp:65: `#define tron_unlikely(x) (x) [[unlikely]]`
h/tron/models/model.hpp:1208: `      if tron_unlikely (cfg.log_intermediates) {`
Evidence strength

confirmed by code

Discriminating check

objdump the visit loop of the largest apply_page_range instantiation at HEAD. If the record/|= code sits between the apply_page_tok inline body and the loop back-edge (the jump back to the loop start, in the fall-through path), the hint moves it to a cold block (a code block placed away from the hot path). If it is already out-of-line (a jump to a block after the function's main body), the hint changes nothing.

Suggested fix

Write if tron_unlikely (stats_enabled) at 1650 and 1680 (and optionally at 854, 1275, 1341), matching model.hpp:1208.

Verifier notes

reach: Discriminating check run on the local clang-19 (the C++ compiler, version 19) -O2 (its optimization level 2) object of model_llama_3p1_70b. The on-state block at self_attention.hpp:1650-1653 is laid out in the fall-through path (99 bytes at 0x2199-0x21fb, skipped by a taken je, the x86 jump-if-equal instruction, when the switch is off). So the hint if tron_unlikely (stats_enabled) would change the layout, as the finding says. The runtron binary the PR body measured was not inspected. Treat the production layout as inferred. Still a nit: one taken branch per visit, well inside the PR's measured band.

evidence: Confirmed by objdump of a HEAD-built object (clang-19, llama-3.1-8b, largest apply_page_range instantiation): the record/path_bits on-state block is one shared 145-byte block placed inline between the merged apply_page_tok return paths and the loop-continuation code, so the off state branches over it on every visit; if tron_unlikely (stats_enabled) at 1650 would let the compiler move it to a cold block. The gain is unmeasured, and the PR's off-state numbers are already inside the band. So this stays a nit.

intent: Confirmed by objdump of today's HEAD build (llama-8b object, clang 19): the off state jumps over a 145-byte inlined record block at +0x2793 on the main AVX visit path. So the hint would change layout. The A/A already shows the cost is below its 0.5-0.7 % TPS resolution, and no document records a decision against the hint. So this stays a consistency nit (model.hpp:1208 idiom), not a defect and not a documented design choice.

R1-M52 The pool-row keying checks degrade to a warning, not a failure, on a one-worker pool nit design choicet/t_llama_unit.cpp:2232
Trigger

A host or slice where app_pool().num_workers() == 1.

Consequence

The two W5 discriminators (the row is the last pool worker, and the split moves the row) are skipped with WARN, a macro of Catch2 (the C++ test framework of the repository). Catch2 prints the warning and does not fail the test. On CI (continuous integration) runners the pool is large, so the checks run. The test cannot tell a reviewer when they did not run.

Where

t/t_llama_unit.cpp:2232, t/t_llama_unit.cpp:2243-2274

Evidence
t/t_llama_unit.cpp:2231-2233: `} else {` / `WARN("pool has one worker: the pool-row keying check cannot discriminate");` ; :2272-2274 `} else {` / `WARN("pool has one worker: the split-moves check is skipped");`
Evidence strength

confirmed by code

Discriminating check

Run t_llama_unit under taskset -c 0,1 (the Linux command that pins a process to the listed CPUs). The output shows the WARN lines and a passing result.

Suggested fix

Keep the WARN (the Catch2 test-framework macro that prints a message and lets the test pass) if developer laptops with 2 CPUs must keep passing the test. Otherwise use REQUIRE(state.self_attn_state.attn.size() >= 2) (the Catch2 macro that fails the test) with the same message. Every CI (continuous integration) runner satisfies it.

Verifier notes

reach: The WARN fallbacks are real (t/t_llama_unit.cpp:2227-2233, 2243-2273). The pool size follows the app cpu list (the tuned table, the SYSTEM_CONFIG environment variable, or --app-cores), not the affinity mask. The smallest tuned entry is 6 cpus = 5 workers. So taskset -c 0,1 will not trigger the WARN. Use SYSTEM_CONFIG="--app-cores 0,1" (2 app cpus = 1 pool worker) as the discriminating check. The unconditional CHECK (the Catch2 macro that records a failure and continues) at :2224-2226 (row = last pool worker) still runs on a 1-worker pool. Only the row-0-empty and split-moves discriminators degrade to WARN.

evidence: Confirmed at HEAD: both W5 discriminators sit behind a pool-size guard whose else branch is Catch2 WARN (t/t_llama_unit.cpp:2226-2233 and 2243-2274). The pool size is app_get_num_cpus() - 1 [h/tron/threading.hpp:861]. So any 2-CPU host or affinity mask silently skips them. On such a host the remaining last-row CHECK (lines 2222-2225) also cannot tell old from new keying. On that host row 0 is the last row.

intent: The WARN fallback is not documented anywhere (README, Note, PR body, W5 reply). So it is an undocumented test gap rather than a design choice. It only removes the production-boundary copy of the W5 check on a 2-CPU host. t/t_attn_stats.cpp:685-730 checks the same row keying on a fixed 3-row pool, and that test fails under the old keying on any host. Suggested fix stays: either REQUIRE(attn.size() >= 2) or a one-line comment saying why 2-CPU hosts must keep passing the test.

R1-M12 With stats on, adjacent workers' small path-bit buffers can share one cache line (the 64-byte unit the CPU cache moves) nit future riskh/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:343
Trigger

TRON_ATTN_STATS=1, decode forwards with few token jobs (e.g. 8 users -> 8-byte inner vectors). path_bits is std::vector<std::vector<uint8_t>> (one heap buffer per pool worker, allocated by resize in begin_forward). glibc (the C library whose malloc hands out heap chunks) has a smallest chunk of 32 bytes. So two workers' 8-byte buffers can sit in one 64-byte line. Every visit does path_bits[tok_ix.i] |= bit (a byte read-modify-write) on the worker's own buffer, and the bit is already set after the first visit of that token job.

Consequence

On-state only: two attention workers whose buffers share a line move it between their caches once per visit (millions of visits per run). The PR's headon arms (the throughput runs of the PR head with TRON_ATTN_STATS=1) show no visible cost at 8 users (+0.39 % / -0.05 % TPS, tokens per second, relative to main with the variable unset). So this is a risk for larger worker counts or different allocators, not a measured defect. The off state is untouched (path_bits is nullptr and the block is skipped).

Where

h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:343, h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1487-1489, h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:456, h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1652

Evidence
343: `  std::vector<std::vector<uint8_t>> path_bits;  // [pool worker][token job]`
456-458: `    for (auto& bits : path_bits) {` / `      if (bits.size() < n_token_jobs) bits.resize(n_token_jobs, 0);` / `    }`
self_attention.hpp:1652: `            path_bits[tok_ix.i] |= attn_stats::path_bit(visit.served);`
[R1-38 Worker and layer indexing after W5 (commit bff317e0d3): row keying by pool worker, attention-index to pool-index mapping, split recording, active_workers, fold cost, per-worker leaves, boundary cases] attn_stats.hpp:343 'std::vector<std::vector<uint8_t>> path_bits;  // [pool worker][token job]'; attn_stats.hpp:456-458 'for (auto& bits : path_bits) {\n      if (bits.size() < n_token_jobs) bits.resize(n_token_jobs, 0);\n    }'; self_attention.hpp:1650-1653 'if (stats_enabled) {\n            stats_tally.record(visit.served, relevant_k_tokens, page::page_size);\n            path_bits[tok_ix.i] |= attn_stats::path_bit(visit.served);'
Evidence strength

plausible

Discriminating check

With TRON_ATTN_STATS=1 print the addresses of path_bits[w].data() for consecutive attention workers (or run perf c2c, the Linux tool that reports cache-line sharing, on the headon arm). Addresses 32 bytes apart confirm line sharing. A headon-vs-unset TPS gap that grows with the worker count confirms the cost.

Suggested fix

Allocate path_bits as one vector with a 64-byte-aligned stride per worker (max token jobs rounded up), or test before set (if (!(b & bit)) b |= bit) so the steady-state visit does a read only.

Verifier notes

reach: The buffers only grow (attn_stats.hpp:457). A prompt forward has one token job per prompt token (full.hpp:2115/2123). So after the first prompt longer than about 40 tokens every worker's buffer sits in its own >= 64-byte glibc chunk, and the decode-written prefix bytes no longer share a line. The risk is limited to processes whose forwards have all had <= 40 token jobs. The PR's headon arms (prompt lengths 1024 and 8192 tokens) never exercised that state. So they neither confirm nor bound it.

evidence: path_bits inner vectors grow to the largest job count of any forward (the first prompt forward, hundreds to thousands of bytes) and never shrink [attn_stats.hpp:456-458, :567-570]. So in production decode adjacent workers' written bytes are at least one buffer length apart and cannot share a cache line. The finding's "8-byte buffers" trigger exists only in a process that never ran a prompt forward (decode-only unit tests). The per-visit private-line RMW (read-modify-write) at self_attention.hpp:1652 remains, and its cost is smaller than the run-to-run variation of the measurement.

intent: Holds as an undocumented on-state future risk, not a design choice. The author put alignas(64) on worker_layer_row and layer_timers for exactly this reason (attn_stats.hpp:224-225, 252). path_bits stayed one small heap buffer per worker, written unconditionally on every visit (self_attention.hpp:1652). The one-line steady-state fix is to test before setting (if (!(bits & bit)) bits |= bit). That turns millions of stores into reads and needs no allocator assumptions. The measured headon cells (-0.05 / +0.70 / +0.39 % TPS at 8 users) show no cost today.

Found by

OFF-STATE COST AND HOT-LOOP CODE (TRON_ATTN_STATS unset must cost nothing measurable; per-visit / per-job / per-forward classification of every production line the PR adds to self_attention.hpp and model.hpp at HEAD 04da001cb5), Worker and layer indexing after W5 (commit bff317e0d3): row keying by pool worker, attention-index to pool-index mapping, split recording, active_workers, fold cost, per-worker leaves, boundary cases

R1-M20 fpga_queries would double count if a plugin planned attention twice in one forward nit future riskh/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:589
Trigger

A plugin (handwritten or generated) that calls assemble_attention_plan more than once inside one run(), for example to re-plan after a split change. Today llama.hpp:596 and the generated run() prologue (TronCpp.hs:1214) call it exactly once per forward. So the condition is not reached.

Consequence

assemble_attention_plan_impl calls construct_hw_plan per minibatch (one group of the forward's token jobs that is planned together) (model.hpp:2313). construct_hw_plan calls note_fpga_query per token job with an FPGA pass (model.hpp:2752). fpga_bits[job] = PATH_BIT_FPGA_4 is idempotent (a repeat has no further effect), but fpga_queries.fetch_add(1) is not. A second plan run adds the same jobs again. note_attn_workers (model.hpp:2303) would also record the split twice (harmless: min/max). Nothing detects the second call.

R1-M38 n_attn_workers_min can read as UINT64_MAX (the largest 64-bit unsigned value) once if two relaxed stores (atomic writes with no ordering guarantee between them) are reordered nit future riskh/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:775
Trigger

First forward of a class: note_attn_workers stores min (from UINT64_MAX) and then max (from 0) as two relaxed stores (atomic writes with memory_order_relaxed, which promise no ordering against other variables) to two different atomics (attn_stats.hpp:472-473). render_forwards guards the min with "max == 0 ? 0 : min" (attn_stats.hpp:775-777), which assumes a reader that sees max > 0 also sees the new min.

Consequence

Relaxed stores to different atomic objects carry no ordering in the C++ memory model. A compiler may emit the max store first. x86 hardware keeps program store order. So this needs compiler reordering. A FUSE read in that window prints n_attn_workers_min = 18446744073709551615 once. Cosmetic, one forward, first forward only.

Where

h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:775, h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:467-474, h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:180-189

Evidence
h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:472-473: "min_relaxed(fc.n_attn_workers_min, n_attn_workers); max_relaxed(fc.n_attn_workers_max, n_attn_workers);"
h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:774-777: "// UINT64_MAX is the \"no forward yet\" initial value of the minimum. load_relaxed(fc.n_attn_workers_max) == 0 ? 0 : load_relaxed(fc.n_attn_workers_min),"
h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:180-189: max_relaxed/min_relaxed are load + store with memory_order_relaxed.
Evidence strength

hypothesis

Discriminating check

Inspect the generated code of note_attn_workers with objdump (the tool that prints a binary's machine code) for the order of the two stores. Or run a reader loop against the first forward under a build with -O3 (the compiler's highest standard optimization level) and look for the UINT64_MAX value. Correct behavior prints 0 or the real minimum only.

Suggested fix

Guard on the field itself: "load_relaxed(fc.n_attn_workers_min) == UINT64_MAX ? 0 : load_relaxed(fc.n_attn_workers_min)" at attn_stats.hpp:775-777. No ordering assumption then remains.

Verifier notes

reach: The concurrent window itself is reachable (unlocked FUSE read callback at attn_stats.hpp:928-940 versus the main-thread store at model.hpp:2303). The wrong value needs the two relaxed stores or loads (atomic operations with memory_order_relaxed, which promise no ordering between them) to be observed out of program order. The C++ model allows that. x86 plus today's GCC and Clang (two C++ compilers) do not produce it. Guarding on the min field itself (UINT64_MAX, the largest 64-bit unsigned value, used here as the 'no value yet' marker) is a one-line fix that removes the assumption.

evidence: The window is symmetric: the reader's two relaxed loads (max at :775, min at :777) may also be reordered under the C++ model. So guarding on the min field itself (== UINT64_MAX) is the right fix regardless of the writer's store order. Only one such window exists per class over the process lifetime. The counters are never reset (attn_stats.hpp:71).

intent: The guard at attn_stats.hpp:775-777 reads the "no forward yet" state from a different atomic (max) than the one that holds the sentinel (min, the 'no value yet' marker). The two are written by two relaxed stores. The standard permits reordering on both writer and reader side. x86 store order and current GCC/Clang practice make the printed UINT64_MAX unobservable on the production target. So this is a one-line cleanup (guard on min == UINT64_MAX), not a defect.

R1-M7 T4 periods are keyed by operation change. Two attention ops on one layer would distort the counts nit future riskh/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:490
Trigger

An ingested model (a model whose per-model plugin code the ingest tool generated) whose schedule has two attention statements on one source layer (LoopyTron.hs:1041 sets attentionModelLayer from the statement's layer, and nextAttentionOpId gives each statement its own op id), or a hybrid model with attention-free layers between attention ops.

Consequence

note_attn_job_entry_w0 closes a period on every op change (attn_stats.hpp:490-498) and adds it to timers_at(cls, t4_last_layer). Two ops on one layer give period_count 2 per forward for that layer and halve the mean. The time of consecutive attention-free layers is counted toward the last attention layer before them. The Note (the design comment block at the top of attn_stats.hpp) defines T4 by operations (attn_stats.hpp:36-38). So the code follows its definition. The leaf name period_cycles_w0 and the README's "layer period" wording assume one op per layer.

Current plugins: llama uses uniform bindings (common.hpp:259-262, op.i == layer.i). No such model was verified either way (Insufficient data: the list of ingested models with a non-uniform op-to-layer map).

Verifier notes

Hand-written plugins use uniform bindings (kv_cache.hpp:448-452). So every shipped model has exactly one op per layer with no gaps. The "layer period" phrase is in the Note at attn_stats.hpp:36 and self_attention.hpp:802.

No configured model has two ops on one layer or attention-free layers. So nothing is miscounted today. Drop the assert suggestion (it would abort production for a legal schedule). R2-M16 rinzler prints the stderr report during static destruction, after the tree is unmounted nit future risksrc/rinzler.cpp:4806
Trigger

rinzler stopped by SIGTERM/SIGINT (a stop from platformd, the host service that manages rinzler, or from systemctl): the loop poll calls stats::unmount() then app().quit() (rinzler.cpp:4801-4808). The --port-file failure path does the same (rinzler.cpp:4759-4761).

The completions_controller that owns every scheduler (rinzler.cpp:1937, 2337) has two owners. One is the local shared_ptr at 4623. The other is the function-local static map of drogon (the HTTP server library), filled through app().registerController(controller) at 4666 (DrClassMap.cc:25-30, 97-103). So ~completions_controller (rinzler.cpp:2457-2465), and with it ~model::state -> ~model_stats -> print_report(stderr) (attn_stats.hpp:388-390), runs only after main returns, during static destruction.

Consequence

The report is not lost by design: print_report uses std::fprintf on stderr and fmt only (attn_stats.hpp:816-848), no spdlog (the logging library), and stderr is open during static destruction. But three limits remain. / (1) The leaves are already gone when the state dies. So the README's 'when the model state is destroyed' event is invisible to any FUSE reader in rinzler, and the only rinzler output is this stderr text. / (2) The report depends on the pre-existing rinzler static-destruction teardown (shutdown_scheduler -> work_barrier on a function-local static work queue, full.cpp:414-418) reaching the attention state without an earlier abort. This review could not run that teardown. / (3) A SIGKILL after systemd's stop timeout drops the report. No code change in this PR alters that order.

Where

Trigger

Consequence

gather_token_job_ids (model.hpp:1796-1816) would still pass. token_job_id_of_token[tok] of a token without a job reads a zero-initialized token_job_id (line 1799-1801 resizes with default values). has_listener[...] = 1 twice is silent. begin_forward's TRON_ASSERT_LE(n_listeners, n_token_jobs) (attn_stats.hpp:452) is only a count bound. A forward with one job carrying two listeners and one job with none would be classified decode_like with no diagnostic.

R2-M7 A mounted leaf re-renders on every read() call. A two-call reader can then get extra tail bytes nit future risksrc/system/fuse_sysfs.cpp:735
Trigger

Stats on, engine under load. A reader (every cat, and any loop that reads until EOF, the end of the file) issues read() #1. op_read serves that call from one rendering. The reader then issues read() #2 at offset = that length to detect EOF. Between the two calls a counter in the leaf crosses a power of ten (visits 999 -> 1000, cycles 10^13, ...). So the second rendering is longer.

Consequence

op_read renders again on the second call and returns content2[offset..]: the last one or more bytes of the newer rendering (for example }\n or \n). The reader then holds {...}\n}\n, which is not valid JSON. get_value() in the unit tests renders once per call and never shows this. Separately, op_getattr renders its own copy for st_size. The mount passes no attr_timeout. So stat/ls -l sizes can lag the content by up to the libfuse (the FUSE user-space library) default attribute cache time. Rate: hypothesis, rare (only when a digit count changes inside the microseconds between two read syscalls).

Where

src/system/fuse_sysfs.cpp:735, src/system/fuse_sysfs.cpp:731, src/system/fuse_sysfs.cpp:623-635, h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:931-934

Evidence
src/system/fuse_sysfs.cpp:725-745: `auto snapshot = node->get_active_data(); value_type value = snapshot->value; ... if (read_callback) { value = read_callback(path, snapshot->value); } std::string content = fs->value_to_string(value) + "\n"; if (offset >= static_cast<off_t>(content.size())) { return 0; } size_t available = content.size() - offset; size_t to_read = std::min(size, available); memcpy(buf, content.c_str() + offset, to_read);` -- the callback runs before the offset check on every call. src/system/fuse_sysfs.cpp:710-711: `fi->direct_io = 1; fi->keep_cache = 0;` (no page cache, every read() reaches op_read). src/system/fuse_sysfs.cpp:623-635: op_getattr calls `read_callback(path, snapshot->value)` again and sets `stbuf->st_size = content.size() + 1`. h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:931-934: the leaf lambda renders live on each call.
Evidence strength

confirmed by code

Discriminating check

On a mounted tree with TRON_ATTN_STATS=1 under load, run for i in $(seq 100000); do cat <mount>/model/<id>/attention/decode_like_layer_0 | python3 -c 'import sys,json; json.load(sys.stdin)' || echo BAD; done. Any BAD line is this behavior. A single-read reader (dd bs=1M count=1) never shows it.

Suggested fix

No code change needed in this PR. Add one README.stats.md sentence. It says: read a leaf with one read() call or tolerate trailing bytes. The reason is that the tree renders live on every read. A fuse_sysfs-level fix (render once per open and keep it behind fi->fh) is a separate change.

Verifier notes

reach: The mechanism is real and reachable (direct_io + per-call re-render + counters that only grow). But it is pre-existing fuse_sysfs behavior shared by every live read-callback leaf (expert_stats.hpp:158/184/192 has the same pattern), and fuse_sysfs.cpp is unchanged by this PR. The failure needs a digit-count change within the microseconds between two read() syscalls. That makes it rare. The shrinking case (offset past the new end) is harmless (returns 0). A one-sentence README note is the proportionate response.

evidence: Holds, with a sharper trigger. A single counter gaining a digit adds one byte. The extra tail is then only "\n", and the result is still valid JSON. A two-call reader gets invalid JSON only when the rendering grows by two or more bytes between its two read() syscalls (two counters changing digit count at once, or a state takeover flipping "{}" to a full rendering). fuse_sysfs.cpp is unchanged by this PR (getattr st_size at :638, direct_io at :710-711 pre-exist). The README sentence is therefore the only in-PR action.

intent: Pre-existing shared FUSE-layer behavior (fuse_sysfs.cpp is not in this PR, and expert_stats and rinzler callback leaves have the same property). A single digit crossing between the two read() calls adds only a trailing "\n". JSON parsers accept that. Invalid JSON needs two or more counters to cross a digit boundary in the same microseconds. A reader that uses a small buffer (dd bs=1) gets bytes from different renderings, the same root cause. The README sentence should therefore say: read a leaf with one read() call.

Triage note

The invalid-JSON read comes from pre-existing render-per-read behaviour of the FUSE layer. This PR's growing leaves can trigger it. Nobody chose that outcome. It is a risk rather than a design choice.

Found by

fuse-mounted-read-path (second round): the mounted FUSE read/getattr path that executes the attn_stats leaf callbacks in rinzler and runtron, the singleton lifecycle, and the enumerators of /model/

R1-M50 Two or three abort forks cannot tell a tron assert from the libstdc++ (the GNU C++ standard library) bounds abort nit test gapt/CMakeLists.txt:599
Trigger

Delete the TRON_ASSERT in token_path_span (worker branch), note_fpga_query or end_forward. t_llama_unit (which now compiles t_attn_stats.cpp) is built with -D_GLIBCXX_ASSERTIONS (the compiler define that turns on the libstdc++ bounds checks). So the following path_bits[worker], fpga_bits[token_job] or fpga_bits[job] access aborts with SIGABRT (the abort signal) anyway.

Consequence

The abort case still passes. But libtron in production has no _GLIBCXX_ASSERTIONS. The deleted assert would therefore be silent undefined behavior there. The other eleven forks are discriminating. For row() a worker of 3 with n_workers 3 and n_layers 4 gives flat index (0*3+3)*4+0 = 12, inside the 24-row vector. So only the TRON_ASSERT can fire. fpga_at / timers_at behave the same. token_path_span's n_token_jobs check and end_forward's size check compare sizes without indexing.

R1-M19 Comment says the forward_scope destructor runs on exception unwinding, but forward() is noexcept nit doc mismatchh/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:864
Trigger

Any exception thrown inside run_forward (not noexcept, model.hpp:1710) or its callees, for example std::bad_alloc from a vector resize, while TRON_ATTN_STATS=1.

Consequence

model::state::forward is declared noexcept (model.hpp:1653). An exception that reaches it calls std::terminate. The C++ standard does not guarantee stack unwinding before terminate in that case, and the common ABIs (application binary interfaces, here the GCC and Clang runtimes) do not unwind. So the sentence 'The destructor also runs when forward() unwinds through an exception, so such a forward is still counted' describes a path that does not exist. No counter is wrong. The comment promises a behavior the code cannot deliver.

Where

h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:864, h/tron/models/model.hpp:1653, h/tron/models/model.hpp:1710

Evidence
attn_stats.hpp:864-866: '// comment W2). The destructor also runs when forward() unwinds through an\n// exception, so such a forward is still counted; tron's asserts abort rather\n// than throw.'  model.hpp:1653: '        ranged_mask const& query_mask_in) noexcept {'  model.hpp:1710-1711: '    void run_forward(\n        indexed_span<token_id, const token_batching_affinity_id> live_groups) {'  assert.hpp:59-62: tron_abort ... std::abort();
Evidence strength

confirmed by code

Discriminating check

Compile a tiny probe: a noexcept function that constructs an object with a printing destructor and then throws. With GCC or Clang the destructor output never appears before the terminate message. Or simply read model.hpp:1653: the noexcept on forward() shows that the sentence describes a path that cannot happen.

Suggested fix

Replace the sentence with: 'forward() is noexcept and tron's asserts abort. So a forward that does not complete is never counted. There is no partial record.' Keep the rest of the paragraph.

Verifier notes

reach: Confirmed by a probe under tron's own compiler (clang 19.1.7 from nix, the package manager that supplies the build toolchain) and g++ 11.4, at optimization levels -O0 and -O2, with and without an outer catch. A local's destructor inside a noexcept function never runs before std::terminate. So the comment's unwinding sentence describes an impossible path. Soften the suggested replacement: say "forward() is noexcept and tron's asserts abort, so a forward that does not complete terminates the process and is never read", not "no partial record". The reason: note_attn_workers and the worker rows are written during the forward.

evidence: Confirmed by a probe built with the repo's own clang++-19 (and g++ 11.4). An exception escaping a noexcept function calls std::terminate with no destructor of that function's locals run. So ~forward_scope never executes on that path. The fix is comment-only. The second half of the sentence (tron's asserts abort, not throw) is correct per h/common/assert.hpp:59-62.

intent: Holds as a comment-only nit. forward() is noexcept [model.hpp:1653]. So an exception escaping run_forward terminates without unwinding, and ~forward_scope never runs. The sentence at attn_stats.hpp:864-866 promises a path that does not exist. This is not a documented design choice (Wade's W2, the README and the PR body say nothing about exceptions). Replace the sentence with "forward() is noexcept and tron's asserts abort, so a forward that does not complete is never counted."

R1-M28 Hardware-join T5 also covers waiting for a peer worker to finish unpacking a pass nit doc mismatchh/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1149
Trigger

FPGA-assisted layer, two join workers share a pass scratchpad. Worker A's try_unpack_hw_pass fails. The cause: worker B holds unpack_state == 1 (mid-decode). A makes no progress and enters the wait loop.

Consequence

The wait loop's ready() predicate (self_attention.hpp:1059-1064) returns false while unpack_state == 1. So the stretch (one continuous T5 wait interval) ends when the peer finishes unpacking, not only when "an FPGA pass or the peer workers' software partials become ready" (attn_stats.hpp:39-41, README.stats.md:285-287, self_attention.hpp:1133-1135). The timer is right (it is pure waiting). The text names two of three causes.

Where

h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1149, h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:39-41, README.stats.md:285-287, h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1133-1135

Evidence
1059: auto ready = [&](size_t pass_ix, size_t batch_job_ix) {
1060:   if (!completed(pass_ix)) return false;
1062-1063:   return scratchpad.unpack_state[batch_job_ix].load(std::memory_order_acquire) != 1;
1091: if (!try_unpack_hw_pass(q_batch, pass_ix, batch_job_ix)) break;
1149: any_ready = ready(groups[d].pass_ixs[cursor.next[d]], batch_job_ix);
Evidence strength

confirmed by code

Discriminating check

The t_llama_unit stream_hw_joins case with pending_decode == true (t_llama_unit.cpp:2918-2965) sets unpack_state 1 on pass 0. With a second thread holding that state for 1 ms before releasing, join_wait_cycles would read > 0 with every pass already complete.

Suggested fix

Add "or a peer worker finishes unpacking a completed pass" to the T5 sentence in attn_stats.hpp:39-41, README.stats.md:285-287 and the comment at self_attention.hpp:1133-1135.

Verifier notes

reach: Holds and is reachable: the third wait cause needs n_kv_heads > 1 with two join workers on different kv heads of the same job (Note at self_attention.hpp:975-979 describes exactly this convergence). The code comment at 1133-1135 sits four lines under the ready lambda whose definition includes "not mid-decode". So it is the least misleading of the three texts. attn_stats.hpp:39-41 and README.stats.md:285-288 are read without that context. They are where the extra clause matters. The cited t_llama_unit case (2918-2965, pending_decode) does NOT show it (the ready job finishes on the first loop iteration, T5 stays 0). A variant with every pass complete, state 1 held by a second thread, and no other progress would show it.

evidence: Confirmed: the ready lambda's own comment (self_attention.hpp:1058, "Completed, and not mid-decode by another worker") already states the third exit cause, so the fix is to carry that phrase into the three T5 sentences; one could argue "pass becomes ready" implicitly means the lambda's definition, but the README reader has no access to that lambda.

intent: Confirmed: the wait loop's ready() predicate (self_attention.hpp:1059-1064, pre-existing base code) also blocks on a peer worker's in-progress unpack (unpack_state == 1). So T5 has three exit causes. The PR's three text locations name two. The extra stretch is bounded by one pass decode (a per-head bf16 copy, bf16 being the 16-bit brain floating-point format). So readings barely change. A one-clause doc fix suffices.

R1-M29 T3 and T4 have different job boundaries. T4 minus T3 also contains between-job plugin work nit doc mismatchh/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:35
Trigger

Consequence

So it excludes what the plugin (the model-specific code around the attention call) does around the call: begin_attention_job's scratchpad barrier and help_drain_gof (self_attention.hpp:761-773), rope_queries (llama.hpp:756-757), permute_vo and the attn_done_barrier in finish_attention_job (llama.hpp:762-770, model.hpp:2147-2151, self_attention.hpp:865-867). R1-M3 A live read during the forward's end can show forwards one ahead of token_jobs nit doc mismatchh/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:577
Trigger

A FUSE reader (the single FUSE worker thread, doc/fuse_stats_design.md:118) reads <class>_forwards while the main thread is inside ~forward_scope. Inside ~forward_scope, note_forward_wall runs first (attn_stats.hpp:881). Then end_forward increments token_jobs_by_path_set once per job (attn_stats.hpp:566-576), and only then adds token_jobs (attn_stats.hpp:581).

Consequence

During that window a read can show forwards = N+1 with token_jobs of N forwards, and sum(token_jobs_by_path_set) larger than token_jobs by up to one forward's jobs. Both are transient and correct after the fold (the per-job loop in end_forward, attn_stats.hpp:566-576). README.stats.md:255-261 and attn_stats.hpp:577-579 document only the three job counts. A reader who computes per-forward averages or checks sum(histogram) == token_jobs from a live read can see a false mismatch.

Where

h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:577, README.stats.md:255-261, h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:540-546, h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:566-584, h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:879, h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:539, h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:553, h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:589, h/tron/models/model.hpp:2747, README.stats.md:256

Evidence

h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:575: "fc.token_jobs_by_path_set[set].fetch_add(1, std::memory_order_relaxed);" (inside the per-job loop, before line 581 "fc.token_jobs.fetch_add(n_token_jobs, ...)")
h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:577-579: "The three job counts move together, in three relaxed adds. A read between them can differ ... A read at any other time sees the equality."

Evidence strength

confirmed by code

Discriminating check

Poll <class>_forwards at high rate during a long prefill forward (a forward over prompt tokens): a sample with forwards == token_jobs-derived count + 1, or sum(token_jobs_by_path_set) > token_jobs, shows the window. A documented statement would make it acceptable.

Suggested fix

Add one sentence to README.stats.md:255-261 and the Note at attn_stats.hpp:577-579: "forwards and wall_cycles are added before the fold, and the histogram before token_jobs. So a read during the fold can show forwards ahead by one, and the histogram sum ahead of token_jobs by one forward's jobs." Optionally add token_jobs before the histogram loop so the histogram never leads it.

Verifier notes

reach: Confirmed at HEAD: the fold loop at attn_stats.hpp:566-576 runs n_token_jobs iterations before token_jobs is added at :581. So the histogram-ahead window is one loop over all jobs of the forward (long for a prefill forward). forwards (:543) leads token_jobs for all of end_forward. README.stats.md:255-261 and the Notes at :447-449 and :577-579 document only the three job counts. fpga_queries (:593) already leads token_jobs during the whole forward by design. So a sentence covering forwards, wall_cycles, fpga_queries and the histogram together is the minimal fix.

evidence: Confirmed at HEAD: forwards/wall_cycles are added before the fold loop, and the path-set histogram is filled inside it. So a FUSE read during ~forward_scope (render_forwards loads each atomic separately, attn_stats.hpp:760-787) can show forwards one ahead of token_jobs, and sum(histogram) > token_jobs by one forward's jobs. The window is the whole fold loop (jobs x attention rows), not "a few instructions". The README and Note make no wrong claim. They only omit this. So nit/doc gap holds. A reorder of token_jobs ahead of the histogram loop would not close the forwards lead. The reason is that note_forward_wall runs first.

intent: Holds as a documentation gap, not a wrong-number defect. The window is only the fold loop inside ~forward_scope (microseconds est.). Every counter is correct once it ends. The README's consistency promise covers only the three job counts. Adopt only the one-sentence doc fix. Moving token_jobs before the histogram loop would break the documented 'three counts move together' property.

R1-M6 'T2 minus T5 is compute plus fold' omits the untimed wait for the output buffer inside T2 nit doc mismatchREADME.stats.md:290
Trigger

Any layer where an attention worker reaches output_channel.start_writing (self_attention.hpp:840) before the main thread has prepared that channel for the layer. In the llama plugin (the tron model implementation in llama.hpp) the next layer's WQ (the query-projection matrix multiply) is launched (llama.hpp:686, async case) before next_layer() (llama.hpp:695) calls vo_channel.prepare() (llama.hpp:1555). prepare() itself waits for the previous layer's WO (the output-projection matrix multiply) to finish reading vo (the buffer that carries the attention output to WO) (common.hpp:540-545, threading.hpp:951-963, llama.hpp:1156-1163).

Consequence

The wait in start_writing (common.hpp:550-552 -> barrier::wait, threading.hpp:966-968, a blocking wait_until_geq) lies inside the T2 window (self_attention.hpp:832 to 853) and is not part of T5. README.stats.md:289-290 and attn_stats.hpp:43 tell the reader that T2 - T5 is compute plus fold. A reader attributes this wait to attention compute. The Note's definition of T2 at attn_stats.hpp:33-35 ("both passes and the join, without the upstream K/V wait") is also incomplete for the same reason. Magnitude not measured. Insufficient data: a perfetto trace (perfetto is the event-tracing tool that tron's TRACE_EVENT macros write to) with the "vo" channel wait would give it.

R1-M65 The Note's memory line omits the path-bit and FPGA-bit scratch vectors nit doc mismatchh/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:83
Trigger

Switch on, a forward with many token jobs (a prefill chunk, a prompt-processing slice of thousands of tokens) on a pool of n_workers (the pool worker count).

Consequence

begin_forward grows every path_bits vector to n_token_jobs bytes and never shrinks it. So the on-state memory is rows + timers + n_workers x max(n_token_jobs) + max(n_token_jobs) bytes. The Note's 'Memory when on' sentence names the first two terms only. For 27 workers and a 8192-token chunk that is about 230 KB more, the same order as the 373 KB rows figure.

Where

h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:83

Evidence
attn_stats.hpp:83-85: `Memory when on: 2 classes x pool workers x layers x 192 bytes of rows, for / example 27 x 36 x 2 x 192 = 373 KB, plus 2 x layers x 64 bytes of timers.` attn_stats.hpp:426-429: `fpga_bits.assign(n_token_jobs, 0); for (auto& bits : path_bits) { if (bits.size() < n_token_jobs) bits.resize(n_token_jobs, 0); }`.
Evidence strength

confirmed by code

Discriminating check

After a forward with N token jobs, path_bits[w].capacity() >= N for every w. The Note's figure does not include it.

Suggested fix

Append 'plus (pool workers + 1) x the largest forward's token jobs in bytes of path-bit scratch'.

Verifier notes

reach: Confirmed: attn_stats.hpp:83-84 counts rows and timers only. begin_forward (attn_stats.hpp:455-458) grows path_bits to (pool workers) x N bytes plus fpga_bits N bytes and never shrinks them. README.stats.md and the PR body state no memory figure.

evidence: Holds at HEAD. The resize evidence is at attn_stats.hpp:455-458 (not 426-429). The scratch is grown for all n_workers pool workers (constructor sizes path_bits to n_workers at :380). So the added term is (pool workers + 1) x the largest forward's token-job count in bytes, kept until the model state is destroyed.

intent: Holds: the Note names the scratch (path bits, FPGA bits) at line 64-65, but the memory sentence at line 83-84 counts rows and timers only. The scratch is (pool workers + 1) x largest n_token_jobs bytes, never shrunk, plus the small fpga counter array (2 x layers x sizeof(layer_fpga_counters)). The 230 KB example assumes a forward of 8192 token jobs. This check did not confirm 8192 as the production maximum. The fix is one appended clause either way.

R1-M82 'prompt chunk' carries two meanings in the listener comment of forward_counters nit doc mismatchREADME.stats.md:250
Trigger

A reader of attn_stats.hpp:286-288 interpreting listener_jobs for a long prompt.

Consequence

The comment says the last token of a prompt chunk has a listener with an empty logits (per-word scores) buffer, and in the next sentence that a chunk cut by the per-forward token limit has no listener in that forward. In the code a listener is attached once per extend request node [src/tron/scheduler/full.cpp:191-192]. An extend request is one request that adds tokens to a user's sequence, and the node is its entry in the token tree (tron's tree of all users' token sequences, distinct from the FUSE tree of leaves). The listener is registered only when the whole edge (the node's run of tokens) was processed in this forward [full.hpp:2455-2459]. So 'chunk' means an extend request in one sentence and a forward cut in the next. The README (README.stats.md:250-252) defines chunk as 'the slice of a prompt that one forward processes'. That definition matches only the second meaning.

Where

README.stats.md:250, h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:286

Evidence
attn_stats.hpp:286-288: `// The last token of a prompt chunk has one too (with an empty logits buffer\n  // when the chunk is not the prompt's last). A chunk cut by the per-forward\n  // token limit gets its listener in the forward that finishes it.`  full.hpp:2455-2456: `if (f.processed_tokens == edge.token_count() &&\n                !n->listeners[is_eagle].empty()) {`
Evidence strength

confirmed by code

Discriminating check

Ask whether a 4096-token prompt sent as one request and cut by live_token_limit into 4 forwards produces 1 listener (code) or 4 (first sentence of the comment).

Suggested fix

Say 'the last token of one extend request (a node) has a listener. A request cut over several forwards by the per-forward token limit registers it in the forward that finishes the node'. Keep 'chunk' for the forward cut as the README does.

Verifier notes

reach: More specific than the finding states: "prompt chunk" is already a tron term (h/libtron.hpp:197 get_prompt_chunk_size, TRON_PER_USER_PROMPT_CHUNK_LIMIT, default 128 tokens, one extend request per chunk from src/tron/generation/context.cpp:42). README.stats.md:250-252 redefines it as the per-forward slice. That redefinition contradicts the tron term. The finding's discriminating check is wrong: a 4096-token prompt sent through the generation loop yields 32 extend requests and 32 listeners by default. The right check is one extend request cut by TRON_LIVE_TOKEN_LIMIT over k forwards. That case increments listener_jobs once, in the finishing forward. Fix: define "prompt chunk" as the libtron extend request in both the comment and the README, and call the live_token_limit case "a partially scheduled edge" or "a forward cut".

evidence: The attn_stats.hpp comment is internally consistent: both sentences use "prompt chunk" in tron's own sense (one extend request of TRON_PER_USER_PROMPT_CHUNK_LIMIT tokens, default 128, h/libtron.hpp:193-204, src/tron/generation/context.cpp:40-49). The mismatch is README.stats.md:250-251, whose parenthetical redefines chunk as the per-forward slice. Fix the README parenthetical to "one extend request of TRON_PER_USER_PROMPT_CHUNK_LIMIT tokens, 128 by default, which one or more forwards process", not the header comment.

intent: The attn_stats.hpp:286-288 comment is consistent: "prompt chunk" there is the code's per-user chunk (TRON_PER_USER_PROMPT_CHUNK_LIMIT tokens, default 128, one extend request with one listener, empty logits unless it ends the prompt) [libtron.hpp:197-204, context.cpp:39-73, full.cpp:191-192]. A chunk that live_token_limit splits registers its listener in the finishing forward (full.hpp:2455-2456). The mismatch is only the README's definition "the slice of a prompt that one forward processes" (README.stats.md:250-252). It should instead say "one user's slice of TRON_PER_USER_PROMPT_CHUNK_LIMIT prompt tokens sent as one request. A forward can carry several chunks or part of one".

R2-M1 Draft-model forwards after a sampling round carry KV-only jobs. The README class rule is target-only nit doc mismatchREADME.stats.md:254
Trigger

Speculation on (legacy_dispatch.cpp:68-73 attaches a speculator scheduler when an eagle model exists). Read the draft/EAGLE model's own attn-stats leaves (a separate model_stats per model state, self_attention.hpp:496-498). Two cases. / (a) Separate draft model: the last draft layer is lazy (speculate.cpp:630-643, lazy_sequence_prompt only records lazy_tokens). When sampling accepts a path ending at a last-layer node, the next draft_step extends from get_location_speculator(current), and its lazy_tokens hold that token. full.cpp:109-115 prepends the lazy tokens to the new edge. The result is a 2-token edge with one listener at its end. / (b) EAGLE: the exact model (tron's name for the target model) sets eagle_ready=false for every token it computed (full.hpp:2441-2443). So the first draft step of each round re-encodes the accepted path's tokens as KV-only jobs with no eagle listener.

Consequence

In the draft model's leaves the first draft step of every round is classified prompt_or_mixed (n_listeners < n_token_jobs), although no prompt is involved and all jobs are generated tokens. README.stats.md:254 says listener_jobs covers 'every generated token', and the Note/PR body describe decode_like as 'one generated token per user'. Both are true for the target model and false for the draft/EAGLE model's stats. The target model's verify forward itself is decode_like (see verified_correct).

R2-M15 README's 'one state per prompt batch' leaves undefined that a batch is the whole prompt set nit doc mismatchREADME.stats.md:270
Trigger

Reader of README.stats.md:270-271 or attn_stats.hpp:69-70 running runtron -u N (N prompts, one per simulated user) and expecting N states or N stderr reports.

Consequence

In runtron the prompt set is split into one batch per prompt only under params.sequential (stream_generate.hpp:1379-1394). Otherwise every prompt of the run is one batch. So -u N alone builds one scheduler, one model::state and prints one report. --iterations K reuses the same scheduler (stream_generate.hpp:934-946: one get_scheduler at 915, K driver calls). So the counters accumulate across iterations into one report. --isolated-iterations K ('restarting scheduler between runs', runtron.cpp:152, loop at stream_generate.hpp:1453-1462) is the flag that builds K states and prints K reports, each with the same 'model <id>' header and no iteration marker. Neither the README nor the Note names these flags.

The per-step page count is the count of ready pages. Its visits are filed under PASS_PENDING_1 (self_attention.hpp:1683-1684). So it can never appear under a ready_ key even when it is full.

R2-M19 Prefill numbers need a 128-token-per-user chunk that the pull request never states nit doc mismatchingest/src/TronCpp.hs:264
Trigger

A reader re-derives the prefill numbers (ready_amx_visits 16,515,072, pending_avx_visits 3,538,944, pending_avx_full_page_visits 1,216,512, token jobs 1,024 'avx' + 7,168 'avx+amx', attn_jobs 5,760) from '8 users x 1024 prompt tokens'.

Consequence

All five numbers follow only when every prefill forward carries 128 tokens of each of the 8 users (1,024 token jobs per forward, 8 forwards). / - ready = 128 queries x 2c ready pages summed over chunks c = 0..7 (sum 2c = 56) x 2,304 = 16,515,072. / - pending visits per (user, layer, head, chunk) = 128 (page 0) + 64 (page 1, queries 64..127 only: queries 0..63 fail the sees_page gate at 1605/1617) = 192, x 8 chunks x 2,304 = 3,538,944. / - full-page AVX visits = 65 (queries 63..127 on page 0) + 1 (query 127 on page 1) = 66, x 8 x 2,304 = 1,216,512. / - attn_jobs = 8 forwards x 36 layers x 20 workers = 5,760 (one run_attention_job per minibatch, and ingested plugins allow one minibatch). A minibatch is one slice of a forward's token jobs that runs as a unit. An ingested plugin is a model plugin that the ingest tool generated from the model's traced graph.. The 128-per-user chunk is not a code constant: it is the ingested plugin's forward budget (max_minibatches 1 x max_minibatch_size 1024 = live_token_limit and capacity_limit at full.hpp:1784/1792) divided by 8 users. 'the first chunk' in the PR body therefore means 8 users' first 128-token chunks. A one-user-per-forward split (1,024 tokens of one user) would give 8 users x 8,704 pending visits per (user, layer, KV head) x 288 (36 layers x 8 KV heads) = 20,054,016 pending visits. So the numbers exclude it. Insufficient data on the mechanism that splits the 1,024 budget evenly across users: the admission loop read (full.hpp:2111-2129, 2331-2338, 2455-2470) stops at live_token_limit but shows no per-user share rule.

R2-M3 For generated plugins the stats layer is the attention-operation ordinal, not the model layer index nit doc mismatchREADME.stats.md:243
Trigger

Any ingested model. TypedFxBulk (the ingest module that converts the model's traced graph) assigns the ModelLayerId of each TronSDPA (one attention operation node of that graph) from a per-function counter. The counter increments once per attention operation. The model's own layer_idx is not used. The generated plugin's n_layers (row count, facts.n_layers) is num_hidden_layers from the config.

Consequence

For every ingested model at HEAD (one attention per hidden layer: qwen-3-4b, gpt-oss, gemma-3/4, mixtral, ...) the ordinal equals the hidden-layer index. So rows, note_fpga_pass filing and the T4 key are correct. The README/Note wording 'per layer', 'model layer' and the leaf key layer_<l> describe the l-th attention operation, which is what the code keys by. A future ingested model with attention-free hidden layers would file the l-th attention op under layer l (not under its hidden-layer index) with no error.

A future explicit-cache model (a plugin that lists its KV cache slot per attention operation) with two attention ops in one hidden layer would have n_attention_ops > n_layers. Then model_layer.i would reach n_layers. row()/timers_at()/fpga_at() abort on that value with the switch on (attn_stats.hpp:395/409/419). The off state is unaffected. The uniform-cache case is caught at compile time (kv slot = model layer, n_slots = n_layers, kv_cache.hpp:375).

Where

Evidence

evidence: Holds as a nit doc mismatch: the stats "layer" key is binding.model_layer.i. ingest assigns it from a per-attention-op counter (TypedFxBulk.hs:199-204, whose own comment calls it "a dumb heuristic"). R2-M9 Size test cites MAX_STRING_LENGTH as a leaf limit, but the read path never applies it nit doc mismatcht/t_attn_stats.cpp:511
Trigger

Reading t_attn_stats.cpp:509-513 or a future contributor sizing a new leaf.

Consequence

The comment says every leaf 'must keep ... below fuse_sysfs::MAX_STRING_LENGTH'. fuse_sysfs (the code module of tron's FUSE layer) enforces that constant only on op_write (returns the -E2BIG error code) and file_handle::set_value (returns false). The callback result in op_read, op_getattr and get_value is delivered whole, and the header says make_file itself allows a longer string. The assertion is a self-imposed budget, not a contract of the FUSE layer. The stated reason is wrong.

Where

Verifier notes

reach: Confirmed: attn_stats leaves are read-callback leaves [attn_stats.hpp:928-932]. fuse_sysfs applies MAX_STRING_LENGTH only on op_write (:804) and set_value (:904), never to callback output in op_read/op_getattr. So the "must ... because" in t_attn_stats.cpp:511-513 names a contract that does not exist. Two corrections to the suggested fix: (1) "fits one FUSE read request" is not the reason either. op_read serves offsets, and FUSE read requests are far larger than 2 KiB. R1-M78 active_workers places the enabled flag in the loop condition instead of an early return nit styleh/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:710
Trigger

Any call of render_summary with the switch off (the summary leaf exists in the off state).

Consequence

Correct (the loop runs zero times when off, and row() would assert on empty rows), but the guard hides in the loop header. Every other method in the file uses if (!enabled) return.

Where

h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:710

Evidence
L708-710: `size_t active_workers() const noexcept {` / `size_t n = 0;` / `for (size_t w = 0; w < n_workers && enabled; ++w) {`
Evidence strength

confirmed by code

Discriminating check

Compare with L741, L762, L792 which guard with if (enabled).

Suggested fix

if (!enabled) return 0; before the loop, then w < n_workers.

Verifier notes

reach: Holds as a style nit, with one correction: the && enabled term is needed for correctness, not redundant. row() asserts only bounds (n_workers/n_layers stay set when off, rows stays empty per L331/L377). So dropping the term would read past an empty vector rather than assert. That is a reason to make the guard an explicit if (!enabled) return 0; as the other methods do (L741, L762, L792, L805).

evidence: Holds as a nit. One correction: without the guard, the off state would not trigger an assert (row() bounds pass, as n_workers keeps its real value). It would instead read past the empty rows vector (undefined behavior). So the && enabled term is needed for correctness. It deserves the explicit if (!enabled) return 0; form the rest of the file uses. The render_* methods use if (enabled) { rather than if (!enabled) return. So "every other method" is slightly overstated.

intent: The && enabled term is needed for correctness, not decorative: rows is only allocated when enabled (L377), and row() asserts against n_workers rather than rows.size() (L398-401). So dropping the guard would read an empty vector in the off state. The suggested if (!enabled) return 0; keeps that protection and matches the guard style of the other 16 enabled-gated methods.

R1-M26 The per-visit path-bit OR also runs for empty visits (a store of an unchanged byte) info design choiceh/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1652
Trigger

Switch on, an empty visit.

Consequence

path_bit(empty) is 0, so line 1652 does byte |= 0: one load and one store per empty visit with no effect. Only in the on state. The measured on-state cost is not visible in the PR's A/A (the PR's comparison of repeated runs of the same setup, whose spread sets the smallest cost the measurement can see). So this is a note, not a defect.

R1-M31 Timers use the non-serializing rdtsc (the CPU instruction that reads the time-stamp counter), so each stamp can move by tens of TSC cycles info design choiceREADME.stats.md:265 (for the suggested README clause, and the primary location h/system/system.hpp:315 is correct)
Trigger

Every timer stamp (hardware::system::rdtsc at system.hpp:315-319 is a bare rdtsc, with no lfence or rdtscp, the two ways to keep the read in program order).

Consequence

Out-of-order execution can place the stamp a few tens of TSC cycles before or after neighbouring loads. T5 stretches of a few hundred TSC cycles carry that jitter. The sums over thousands of jobs do not. This is the convention of the whole file (attn_elapsed, compute_elapsed, speedometer) and of the calibration code. So it is not a change request.

Where

README.stats.md:265 (for the suggested README clause, and the primary location h/system/system.hpp:315 is correct), h/system/system.hpp:315

Evidence
315-319: inline uint64_t rdtsc(void) noexcept { unsigned int lo, hi; asm volatile("rdtsc" : "=a"(lo), "=d"(hi)); return ((uint64_t)hi << 32) | lo; }
Evidence strength

confirmed by code

Discriminating check

Not needed. This is a known property of rdtsc. A note in the README's "Cycle counters are TSC ticks" sentence would tell readers the resolution.

Suggested fix

None required. Optionally one clause in README.stats.md:279 ("Cycle counters are TSC ticks"): "read with rdtsc, so single stamps carry tens of cycles of jitter".

Verifier notes

reach: Holds as an informational note. All T1-T5 stamps go through the bare rdtsc at system.hpp:315-319, and no serialized variant exists in the tree. The optional README clause belongs at README.stats.md:265 (not :279). Per-stamp jitter of tens of TSC cycles matters only for the smallest T5 stretches. The summed counters are unaffected.

evidence: Holds as an informational note: rdtsc at system.hpp:315-319 is pre-existing code outside the PR, and every PR timer (T1-T5) and the pre-existing attn_elapsed/compute_elapsed timers use it the same way. The README sentence the fix targets is at README.stats.md:265, not :279.

intent: Holds as an informational design note. The PR reuses the repo-wide bare rdtsc (system.hpp:315), the same read the TSC calibration (system.cpp:1780-1782) and the speedometer (model.hpp:2149) use. So no change is warranted. The optional README clause belongs at README.stats.md:265 ("Cycle counters are TSC ticks"), not line 279 as the finding says.
