R1-M34 fpga_query_passes counts logical passes, not physical hardware subcommands info design choiceh/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:265
Trigger

A pass whose shard span needs head tiling or more than one EMEM window: hw_pass.physical_subpasses > 1 (model.hpp:2726-2727). A shard is a fixed-length token slice of the KV cache on the card, and the shard span is the range of shards one pass covers. Head tiling splits the heads into smaller groups. EMEM is the FPGA memory that holds the intermediate Q-K dot products during hardware attention (libpos.hpp:129).

Consequence

note_fpga_pass files one (pass, query) pair per logical hw_pass. The card executes physical_subpasses commands for it. A reader comparing fpga_query_passes with FPGA command counters (or with the per-subpass decode work in try_unpack_hw_pass) sees fewer passes. Neither the hpp comment nor the README says which notion of pass is counted. Not a defect: the K-token count is the same either way.

Where

h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:265, h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:636, h/tron/models/common.hpp:748, h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:271

Evidence
self_attention.hpp:636: stats->note_fpga_pass(layer.i, fpga_k_tokens, B);  (called once per pi in the loop at self_attention.hpp:575 'for (size_t pi = 0; pi < workload.passes.size(); ++pi)')
common.hpp:748: size_t physical_subpasses = 1;               // EMEM-safe HW executions to join
attn_stats.hpp:271-273: '(the inclusive last token index + 1 per (pass, query)) and the number of (pass, query) pairs.'
Evidence strength

confirmed by code

Discriminating check

On a long-context FPGA run where hwattn_plan_cmd_shape returns subcmds > 1, compare fpga_query_passes with the count of attn_launcher commands in the perfetto (the tracing tool that records tron's timing events) 'hwattention' track. The stats value is smaller by the subpass factor.

Suggested fix

State in the attn_stats.hpp comment at 271-273 and in the README sentence proposed above that a pass is one hw_pass of the plan (one device, one shard window), whatever number of physical subcommands it takes.

Verifier notes

reach: Holds as a documentation nit, with two corrections. Head tiling does NOT create extra subcommands (a tiled span is one physical command, libpos.hpp:212-213). subcmds > 1 comes only from an untiled span wider than the EMEM shard cap (EMEM = the FPGA on-card memory that holds the intermediate Q-K scores; a shard = one 1024-token slice of the KV cache; libpos.hpp:215, e.g. more than 4 shards at 32 heads). The proposed perfetto check cannot show the gap. All subcommands of a pass run inside one attn_launcher (self_attention.hpp:716, worker.cpp:894-896). The discriminating number is sum over passes of pass.physical_subpasses * B (B = the number of query activations batched into one hardware command, at most 32; model.hpp:2729) versus query_passes.

evidence: Holds as a doc/design note, with two corrections. Head tiling that engages yields ONE physical command (libpos.hpp:213-215). So physical_subpasses > 1 needs covered_shards above the EMEM cap with tiling off or no legal tile. The perfetto attn_launcher_cb event (self_attention.hpp:716) is per logical pass too. So the discriminating check must count the worker's num_hw_subcmds (src/pos/worker.cpp:893) or acmds, not launcher events. The quoted comment is at attn_stats.hpp:265-267.

intent: Holds as an undocumented unit choice, with two corrections. Head tiling collapses a long span into ONE physical command (libpos.hpp:212-215). So physical_subpasses > 1 needs the span past the untiled EMEM cap with tiling off or no legal tile (at most 4 physical subpasses per pass, libpos.hpp:37). The perfetto 'hwattention' events are per logical pass (self_attention.hpp:716 attn_launcher_cb 'pass', pi). So the check must compare against driver-level counts (worker.cpp:893-898 num_hw_subcmds / :1207 result blocks per Q), not that track. attn_stats.hpp:123 already ties the count to hw_workload.passes. Only a 'whatever number of physical subcommands it takes' clause is missing at attn_stats.hpp:271-273 and README.stats.md:267.

R1-M44 Off state may pay one constant byte store per visit for page_tok_result::served info design choiceh/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1645
Trigger

Switch off, any visit. apply_page_tok is TRON(inline) (always_inline) with three return points that each write a compile-time-constant served (amx at 1898, empty at 1950, avx at 1982). The only consumer is the if (stats_enabled) block at 1650-1653, and stats_enabled is a runtime value. So the compiler must keep served alive from the return point to the test. The one exception is a compiler that duplicates the block into each return path.

Consequence

If the compiler keeps served in a stack slot, the off state executes one movb $const, N(%rsp) per visit in addition to the one bool test. This is a private stack write, not a shared-memory write and not an atomic. So it does not break the 'no memory write to shared data' intent. But it is one instruction more than 'one bool test' if it happens. The PR's A/A (resolution ~0.5-0.7 %, the run-to-run spread of the measurement) cannot see it either way.

Where

h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1645, h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1898, h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1950, h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1982

Evidence
1645: `          const page_tok_result visit = apply_page_tok<geometry>(was_valid, page,`
1650-1652: `          if (stats_enabled) {` / `            stats_tally.record(visit.served, relevant_k_tokens, page::page_size);` / `            path_bits[tok_ix.i] |= attn_stats::path_bit(visit.served);`
1898: `          return {range_hint, int(page::page_size), attn_stats::visit_path::amx};`
1950: `        return {range_hint, 0, attn_stats::visit_path::empty};`
1982: `      return {range_hint, relevant_k_tokens, attn_stats::visit_path::avx};`
1837-1839: `    TRON(inline)` / `    page_tok_result apply_page_tok(bool was_valid,`
Evidence strength

confirmed by code

Discriminating check

Run objdump -d (objdump = the GNU tool that prints the machine instructions of a compiled binary) on the largest apply_page_range instantiation at HEAD and look at the three inlined return points of apply_page_tok. A movb $0x1/$0x2/$0x0, N(%rsp) (a one-byte store to the stack, or a register move kept live to the test) before the jump to the common if (stats_enabled) test confirms the store. Three duplicated if (stats_enabled) tests with constant-folded record() calls refute it.

Suggested fix

No code change is needed unless the objdump shows the store and someone wants it removed. In that case move the if (stats_enabled) record into each return path of apply_page_tok via a small inline hook that takes the constant path. The constant then folds away when off.

Verifier notes

reach: objdump of the HEAD build (clang 19, RelWithDebInfo = the CMake build type with optimization plus debug symbols, AMX-eligible h128/g4 bf16 instantiation: h128 = head size 128, g4 = 4 query heads per KV head, bf16 = 16-bit brain floating point) shows no stack store of served: the constant is packed into the register that already carries relevant_k_tokens, costing 2 register instructions on the AVX return (movabsq+orq), 0 on the AMX and empty returns, plus the one cmpb $0,0xf(%rsp) bool test. No memory write. Below A/A resolution. Measured on this build only, and the -O3 deb build (the Debian package build, compiled at optimization level -O3) may differ.

evidence: objdump of the HEAD build (llama-3-8b dtensor apply_page_range, clang 19 -O3) shows no stack store of served. Instead the compiler packs served into %rax (the return register) before the (5x duplicated) stats_enabled test. So in the off state an AMX visit pays 2 extra register instructions (movabs+add), an AVX visit 2 (movabs+or), and an empty visit 0, with no memory write. The suggested fix (fold record() into each return path so the constant dies when off) would remove those 2 instructions per visit. The cost is far below the A/A resolution and needs no action.

intent: Objdump of HEAD binaries (clang-19, RelWithDebInfo, AMX-eligible and non-AMX instantiations) shows no stack store of served: the compiler packs it into the rax return register with relevant_k_tokens, and each return path pays 0 to 2 register-only instructions plus the one cmpb $0, N(%rsp) bool test the contract already names. Not checked: the Release (-O3) deb preset. The objdump-check.sh recipe on that runtron would close it.

R1-M46 The 64-byte visit tally is zero-initialized on every apply_page_range call, stats on or off info design choiceh/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1483
Trigger

Every apply_page_range call, switch on or off (about workers x kv_heads x layers x 2 passes calls per forward).

Consequence

Eight 8-byte stores to the stack per call with the switch off. The Note documents this ('one stack tally'). It is per call, not per visit. The compiler cannot elide it. The reason: the reads at 1651/1683 are behind a runtime bool. Cost is far below the A/A resolution (A/A = the difference between two runs of the same binary, the measurement noise band).

Where

h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1483, h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:197-217

Evidence

attn_stats.hpp:15: `// ... Per call the cost is one byte load and one stack tally.`
Evidence strength

confirmed by code

Discriminating check

objdump the prologue of the largest apply_page_range instantiation for 8 zeroing stores (or a 64-byte vector zero) executed before the if (stats_enabled) test.

Suggested fix

None required. If wanted, declare the tally inside the on-state scope by moving the whole per-call setup into an if (stats_enabled) block with an optional-like wrapper. The gain is not measurable.

Verifier notes

reach: Holds as a documented design choice: the 64-byte zero-init at self_attention.hpp:1483 runs on every apply_page_range call (run_sections:1176, every worker, layer and pass) with the switch off, and the Note at attn_stats.hpp:15 budgets exactly this ('one stack tally'). The 'compiler cannot elide it' clause and the 'eight 8-byte stores' form are unverified (no objdump run). The cost is not measurable against the A/A band either way.

evidence: Confirmed at HEAD: the 64-byte tally at self_attention.hpp:1483 is zeroed once per apply_page_range call before the switch is tested, and the Note (attn_stats.hpp:15) already declares it. 'The compiler cannot elide it' is overstated. The tally never escapes (add_visits is inline, const&). So the optimizer may sink the zero-stores into the enabled branch. Only the objdump check decides. The cost is negligible either way.

intent: Confirmed by objdump of the HEAD build (t_llama_unit.cpp.o, RelWithDebInfo). The tally zeroing is one 64-byte vmovdqa64 %zmm0,0x500(%rsp) store (one vector store to the stack), not eight 8-byte stores. It sits between the enabled byte load and the cmp $0x1,%cl switch test. So it runs on every apply_page_range call with the switch off. This is exactly what the Note in attn_stats.hpp:15 documents ("one byte load and one stack tally"). So it is a documented design choice, not a defect.

R1-M37 The destructor and stderr report can run on the FUSE thread when a read overlaps state destruction info future riskh/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:388
Trigger

runtron keeps the FUSE tree mounted until main exits (src/runtron.cpp:485 unmount_guard), and destroys the model state after each prompt batch (h/runtron/stream_generate.hpp:1462-1466). A FUSE read of any attention leaf executes weak.lock() (attn_stats.hpp:931). If the main thread drops the last strong reference (self_attention::state member, self_attention.hpp:477) after that lock succeeded, the FUSE worker thread becomes the last owner.

Consequence

The render finishes on a live object (no use-after-free). Then the shared_ptr in the callback releases. ~model_stats runs on the FUSE worker thread (attn_stats.hpp:388-390). print_report writes the [attn-stats] lines (log lines that carry the PR's [attn-stats] prefix) to stderr from there, after and possibly interleaved with runtron's own end-of-run output.

The FUSE design note asks callbacks to be quick (doc/fuse_stats_design.md:96). The report is a few KB of fprintf.

No data race: the report reads relaxed atomics (atomic loads with no ordering guarantee) of an object no other thread references any more.

Where

h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:388, h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:472-477, src/system/fuse_sysfs.cpp:940-947, doc/fuse_stats_design.md:96

h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:930-932: "[weak, render](...) { if (auto s = weak.lock()) return fuse::fuse_sysfs::value_type(render(*s)); ..."
src/system/fuse_sysfs.cpp:731-735: "{ std::shared_lock<std::shared_mutex> lock(node->structure_mutex_); read_callback = node->read_callback_; } if (read_callback) { value = read_callback(path, snapshot->value); }" (the callback runs outside every lock, on the FUSE thread)
src/runtron.cpp:485: "tron::fuse::stats::unmount_guard fuse_guard;" (unmount only when main returns)
Evidence strength

confirmed by code

Discriminating check

Run runtron with TRON_ATTN_STATS=1 and --isolated-iterations 2 while a shell loop reads <prefix>/decode_like_totals continuously. A report line appearing after the next batch's "attention path stats on" info line (or from a thread id other than main under strace -f, the system-call tracer) shows the destructor ran on the FUSE thread. Correct-by-design behavior would still print once per state.

Suggested fix

Accept and document in the Note (attn_stats.hpp:69-73): "the report prints when the last owner drops. A FUSE read that overlaps the state's destruction prints it from the FUSE thread." If a fixed thread is wanted, print from ~self_attention::state (call stats->print_report(stderr) there and make ~model_stats empty). The report then always runs on the destroying thread.

Verifier notes

reach: Confirmed reachable in runtron only (rinzler keeps one state for the process lifetime). The window is the render time of a single leaf read that overlaps the end of a stream_generate batch. The consequence is limited to the [attn-stats] report printing from the FUSE thread after runtron's batch output. The Note in attn_stats.hpp:69-73 says "One summary goes to stderr when the model state is destroyed" and does not say which thread. The suggested doc sentence or printing from ~self_attention::state both close it.

evidence: Holds at HEAD: the only strong owner is self_attention::state::stats (self_attention.hpp:475), and the FUSE read callback's weak.lock() (attn_stats.hpp:931) can become the last owner. So ~model_stats and its fprintf report (attn_stats.hpp:388-390, 815-849) can run on the FUSE thread. The state dies per batch at stream_generate.hpp:998 (shutdown_scheduler), not at the caller lines 1462-1466 the finding cites. It is memory-safe as written. So it stays info-level. The fix is a Note sentence at attn_stats.hpp:70-73 or printing from ~self_attention::state.

intent: Holds as an undocumented side effect of the documented weak_ptr scheme: the window is one render (microseconds) per state teardown, and it needs a reader polling a leaf at that instant. print_report calls no fuse:: or spdlog (the logging library) function. So the FUSE-thread destructor cannot deadlock or corrupt anything. README.stats.md:245-246 ("a batch runtron run ends before the tree can be read") understates this: a reader polling during the run can read every leaf, and can also become the printer of the report.

R1-M41 render_summary reads the CPU frequency from a plain uint64_t written by the calibration thread info future riskh/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:733
Trigger

Consequence

detail::cpu_frequency_hz is declared as a plain uint64_t (src/system/system.cpp:1794) and returned without synchronization (system.cpp:1897-1899). In the C++ memory model a concurrent plain write and read is a data race. On x86 a 64-bit aligned load is a single access. So the practical effect is at most a stale tsc_hz value.

This is pre-existing infrastructure (the same accessor serves cycles_to_ns everywhere). The PR only adds one more reader.

The writer side (system.cpp:1799-1803 comment about publish order) was not read for an atomic_ref.

Evidence strength

confirmed by code

Discriminating check

intent: The writer is a plain store (src/system/system.cpp:2019). So the formal race is confirmed. It is pre-existing infrastructure shared by every cycles_to_ns caller (h/system/system.hpp:267-271). The repo already documents the same class of race for latest_tsc/latest_sys_ns (system.cpp:1975-1992). intent: The repo's own comments say platformd's per-instance collector scans the whole mount root (src/system/fuse_tronstats.cpp:170-175 and :235-236). So the collector does reach the attention/ string leaves. How it treats an undescribed string leaf is the only unknown. The same unexported JSON-string shape (expert_stats.hpp:178, :185 under /model/<id>/experts/router_N/) has been on main since 2026-07-27 (fdafb7f2dc) with no in-repo record of collector errors or unexpected pho tron stats rows.

fuse-mounted-read-path (second round): the mounted FUSE read/getattr path that executes the attn_stats leaf callbacks in rinzler and runtron, the singleton lifecycle, and the enumerators of /model/

R2-M8 op_read and op_getattr do not catch a throwing read callback. The unit-test path does info future risksrc/system/fuse_sysfs.cpp:737
Trigger

A render_* call on the FUSE thread throws. The only throw site is allocation inside fmt::format_to into a std::string (std::bad_alloc). tron asserts abort rather than throw.

Consequence

The exception leaves op_read/op_getattr. These are static C++ functions called from C code in libfuse (the C library that implements the FUSE file-system side). That C code has no unwind tables (the data a C++ exception needs to pass through a function). So the process terminates instead of the read failing. The unit-test path (file_handle::get_value) catches and returns the stored value. So tests cannot show the asymmetry.

This is pre-existing for every callback leaf (the expert_stats leaf router_N/counts, which reports the expert-router counts, uses the same shape). This PR adds between 5 (summary plus the totals and forwards leaves of both classes) and 2*(2+n_layers+n_workers)+1 (with every layer and worker row present) such leaves per model state.

Where

src/system/fuse_sysfs.cpp:737, src/system/fuse_sysfs.cpp:632, src/system/fuse_sysfs.cpp:944

Evidence
src/system/fuse_sysfs.cpp:736-737: `if (read_callback) { value = read_callback(path, snapshot->value); }` (no try). src/system/fuse_sysfs.cpp:632-633: same in op_getattr. src/system/fuse_sysfs.cpp:944-950: `if (cb) { try { return cb(path_, snapshot->value); } catch (...) { return snapshot->value; } }`. h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:722-737: render_summary is not noexcept and uses `fmt::format_to(std::back_inserter(out), ...)`.
Evidence strength

confirmed by code

Discriminating check

Read the three call sites: the asymmetry is visible in the code. Reachability needs an allocation failure on the FUSE thread. No in-repo test provokes it.

Suggested fix

Out of scope for this PR. If wanted, wrap the callback in op_read/op_getattr the way get_value does, in fuse_sysfs.cpp.

Verifier notes

reach: The uncaught-callback asymmetry is real (fuse_sysfs.cpp:632-633, :734-735 vs :944-948). But op_read/op_getattr already build std::string outside any try for every leaf (fuse_sysfs.cpp:635, :737). So a bad_alloc (the C++ exception thrown when memory allocation fails) on the FUSE thread terminates the process with or without this PR's callbacks. The only reachable trigger is heap exhaustion, and fuse_sysfs.cpp is outside the PR. Not a PR defect. Pre-existing, informational.

evidence: Holds exactly as stated at HEAD. The only throw site is std::bad_alloc inside fmt::format_to (format strings are literals, TRON_ASSERT aborts). So this is a pre-existing fuse_sysfs.cpp property that the PR only multiplies in leaf count, not a defect introduced by the PR. The "without unwind tables" parenthetical does not matter. With or without them, no handler exists on the libfuse thread. So the process terminates either way.

intent: Holds and is pre-existing in the FUSE layer (expert_stats and rinzler callbacks share the same shape). The only throw in the PR's renderers is std::bad_alloc from fmt::format_to into a std::string. So the practical exposure is an out-of-memory FUSE read, and that read would terminate the process either way. The "without unwind tables" phrase is unverified but irrelevant. No handler exists on the libfuse thread. So std::terminate follows regardless.

Found by

fuse-mounted-read-path (second round): the mounted FUSE read/getattr path that executes the attn_stats leaf callbacks in rinzler and runtron, the singleton lifecycle, and the enumerators of /model/

Consequence

This confirms the PR body's claim: main's copy of the test miscompiles under -DTRON_AMX_DISPATCH=ON. R1-M81 The assertion_signal test helper is now in namespace tron, not in an anonymous namespace info stylet/assertion_signal.hpp:13
Trigger

Any test that includes t/assertion_signal.hpp (three binaries).

Consequence

The two copies it replaces were in anonymous namespaces (t_compute_attention_unit.cpp, t_llama_unit.cpp). Follow-up: the five test gaps closed

The five should-fix test gaps were closed in this session, after the review above was written. One implementer agent wrote each test against the production boundary named in the finding, ran a deliberate break of the production code to confirm the new assertion fails (a mutation check), reverted the break, and committed. Five reviewer agents then checked each commit with the test questions of the tron-code-review skill (the review checklist used for tron pull requests). Two asked for changes: R1-M8 had bare literals where the C++ guide (the repository's C++ coding guide) asks for named values, and R1-M1 counted listener notifications in a plain integer that two pool workers increment at once (a data race in the test). Both were fixed in follow-up commits. The implementer pushed 4094b51dde before the review step, against its instructions. The two follow-up changes were therefore pushed as new commits (4c70f04604, 0f784c44ff) on top of it. No history was rewritten. Every commit touches test files only: git diff 04da001cb5..0f784c44ff -- h/ is empty.

What the new tests do not discriminate

  • R1-M15: Two other cases still call note_forward_wall alone (timers case line 278, report-rule case line 841) and assert the same 'forwards is counted in note_forward_wall' detail. They were not in the spec and were left untouched. This is recorded in the commit body.
  • R1-M2: Not discriminated: indexing by tok_ix.i versus batch position. The consumer batch's jobs are 0..31, equal to their batch positions. The spec's suggestion to run the producer batch once more would not close it either. The histogram fold counts one entry per job regardless of which job index carries the bit. So both indexings give the same histogram.
  • R1-M8: Not discriminated: filing T3 for every worker instead of worker 0. The reason: the section's split has one attention worker. Line numbers quoted from the mutation runs were taken before the formatter ran. In the committed file the blocks start at :792 and :847.
  • R1-M9: The fake shards allocate no HBM (high-bandwidth memory on the FPGA card): their kv_slot_extent is 0. So the shard destructor frees nothing. The slot entry is marked present with allocation_bytes 1. The call site only asserts that value to be > 0. static_assert(hardware::shard_tokens == 1024) ties the expected numbers to that shard size.
  • R1-M1: The page is passed in pages. So estimate_attention_work and finish_sw_plan run for real on the one page. The fixture's run launches no workers. So no attention job executes. The listener buffers are empty (std::monostate). So run_logits skips the wcls matmul (the matrix multiply with the output-word weight matrix, n_logit_chks 0). Only the empty-listener notifications run on the app pool.

Full runs at the new head

At 0f784c44ff the four test binaries were rebuilt and run three times each (TRON_ATTN_STATS unset, 1, yes) and t_heterogeneous_scheduler also with USE_HW_ATTN=1 (the setting that enables the fake hardware attention device), all from the worktree root on claude-box (the container this session runs in). All runs passed.

  • t_page_share_counters: 61 assertions in 4 cases.
  • t_amx_dispatch_dtype: 1,612 assertions in 1 case (was 1,600).
  • t_heterogeneous_scheduler: 2,783 assertions in 3 cases (was 2,715 in 2).
  • t_llama_unit: 220,158 assertions in 61 cases (was 62 cases: one hook-level case deleted, its assertions replaced by the new path-set checks). Wall times 0.2 to 2.8 s per run.

Posted test data of 2026-09-30

The two posted comments (test data of 2026-09-29, qwen-3-4b (the model) with tp2 (tensor parallelism across 2 cards), 8 users, prompt 1024 tokens, 256 generated tokens, CPU attention) are arithmetically consistent with the code at HEAD 04da001cb5. Every key, label and leaf name renders at HEAD. The counts computed from formulas, the T1..T5 timer ratios and the worker-0 time decomposition (36xT2 + 36x(T3-T2) + 35x(T4-T3) + 0.61 ms = T1 within 0.1 %) all hold. One correction about the run's source. The run log names the binary as tip bff317e0d3, started 23:24:20Z, 33 minutes before the head commit 04da001cb5 (23:57:08Z). The live snapshot shows listener_jobs 1,144 ahead of token_jobs 1,136, the pre-head behaviour. End-of-run values are the same under both commits (commit message). So the numbers remain valid, but the comment's 'run of the head' does not. Four items remain, all wording. / The comment names the wrong commit. / The worker-leaf 'snapshot at forward 142' is a 56-file sequential read spanning about 240 ms (rows read 17 to 19 forwards later). / 'stored K and V only' hides that kv_only jobs run attention as queries. / attn_stats.hpp:3-4 still says 'per attention worker' while rows are keyed by pool worker.

On 2026-09-30 the author posted three comments on the PR: a run of the head on delphi-3bda, the Intel test machine (the qwen-3-4b model, tp2 = tensor parallel over 2 accelerator cards, 8 users, prompt 1024, 256 generated tokens, CPU attention with USE_HW_ATTN=0, the setting that turns the FPGA attention off, and TRON_ATTN_STATS=1), the leaf samples of that run, and the definitions that changed in the last five commits. This section checks the posted numbers against each other and against the code at the head. The arithmetic was recomputed with a script (every derived number in the comments recomputes from the raw counts). In the tables below, 'orchestrator' names the agent that coordinated this review and ran that script, and 'pass (orchestrator)' marks its checks. Four reading passes (called lenses below, each reads the code from one angle) then checked the code-side claims. Two refuters (passes that try to disprove each finding) checked each resulting finding. Verdict words in the claims table: supported_by_both = the posted data and the code at the head both support the claim. supported_by_data or supported_by_code = only the posted data, or only the code, was checked. contradicted = the evidence shows the opposite. not_verifiable = the posted comments do not hold the needed numbers. In the findings, confirmed_by_data = the reviewer quoted the run files that show it.

Idle time no timer covers

In the posted decode data each attention worker is inside T2 (busy time of one attention job, self_attention.hpp:820-853) for 36 x 193.4 us = 6.96 ms of the 12.51 ms forward, 55.7 %. The other 44.3 % (5.54 ms per forward per worker) is outside every PR timer on attention workers 1 to 19. The leaves of those workers carry only busy_cycles and join_wait_cycles. T5 (4.09 us per job, 2.1 % of T2) is inside T2 and covers none of it. Only worker 0 exposes the idle, and only as differences. / T3 - T2 = 57.8 us per job (2.08 ms per forward, 16.6 %): the latch wait for the roped queries (queries after rotary position encoding) at self_attention.hpp:811-813 and the upstream K/V wait at :830. / T4 - T3 = 81.5 us per layer (2.85 ms per forward, 22.8 %): permute_vo (llama.hpp:765), the attn_done_barrier (self_attention.hpp:866), and begin_attention_job plus rope_queries waiting on the next layer's Q (query vectors) (llama.hpp:754-757). / The head and tail of the forward, 0.61 ms (4.9 %): before the layer-0 job entry and after the last job (the last layer's WO (attention output projection) and FFN (feed-forward network), listener launch at model.hpp:1768-1769, run_logits at :1770, and the release of the latch arena (the memory pool that holds the forward's latches)). The identity 36 x T2 + 36 x (T3 - T2) + 35 x (T4 - T3) + 0.61 ms = 12.51 ms holds within 0.1 %.

Note that T3 - T2 mixes the 20-worker mean T2 with worker 0's T3. Worker 0 (pool worker 7) reads 184.9 us per job in the snapshot. So its own in-job wait is nearer 66 us.

No timer of the PR attributes the prefill idle either. There the join wait (T5) is 345 us per job, 10.2 % of T2, and the per-worker prefill leaves were not posted.

Findings on the comments and the PR body

TD-1 The posted run is of bff317e0d3, not of the head 04da001cb5 should fix doc mismatch

Trigger

A reader takes comment 5902483127 ('CPU-attention runtron run of the head', 'head 04da001cb5') as a runtron test of the head commit.

Consequence

The run started 2026-09-29T23:24:20Z. The head commit is dated 23:57:08Z, 33 minutes later. The binary tip is bff317e0d3.

End-of-run values are the same under both commits. The head commit only moves two fetch_adds (atomic increments) into end_forward and says 'The end-of-run values agreed'. So every posted number remains valid.

The one change of 04da001cb5 (the three job counts move together) is covered by the unit case at t/t_attn_stats.cpp:369-378 and by no runtron run.

The live snapshot even shows the pre-head behaviour (listener_jobs 1,144 ahead of token_jobs 1,136).

Where

Evidence
Header: 'bin=/var/tmp/jhan/tron-attn-head/gen/runtron.attnhead4 tip=bff317e0d3 2026-09-29T23:38:28Z' (cpu2) and '... tip=bff317e0d3 2026-09-29T23:24:20Z' (cpu). The posted wall_cycles 8,610,367,272 is found only in the cpu run (grep over attnstats-20260929*). git log: 04da001cb5 2026-09-29T16:57:08-07:00, bff317e0d3 13:18:29-07:00. Discriminating check

Read the tip= field of the results header, or check whether a mid-forward read can show listener_jobs > token_jobs (pre-head) vs never (head, README.stats.md:257-261).

Suggested fix

In the comment, replace 'run of the head' with 'run of bff317e0d3 (one commit before the head). The head commit changes no end-of-run value, see its message.' Optional: one runtron run of 04da001cb5 with a mid-forward read that shows listener_jobs + kv_only_jobs == token_jobs.

Verifier notes

Supersedes lens finding KEYS-2. KEYS-2 bounded the commit to bff317e0d3..04da001cb5, and both refuters rated it info. The run log settles it. Two refuters said a commit-id proof would add nothing. Their reason was that the values agree. That holds for the numbers, not for the sentence.

TD-2 Worker-leaf 'snapshot at decode forward 142' is a sequential 56-file read spanning about 240 ms. Rows are 17 to 19 forwards past the label nit doc mismatchPR comment 5902523541, worker-leaves paragraph; ~/workspace/intel-AMX/exec/attnstats-20260929-w1/cpu-cell.sh:23-24 (the read loop); h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:926-933 (one read callback per leaf); README.stats.md:264-265 ('an interval is the difference of two reads')
Trigger

A reader treats the posted worker rows (or any multi-leaf read of /model/<id>/attention/) as one instant and computes per-forward numbers from them.

Consequence

Worker 7 reads 5,817 jobs = 161.6 forwards x 36 layers. Worker 26 reads 5,764 = 160.1 forwards. A row at forward 142 could hold at most 5,148 jobs. Dividing a row by 142 gives 41 jobs per forward. That is more than the 36 layers. It looks like a counter bug. Rates per job are unaffected (busy 184.9 us on worker 7 and 182.5 us on worker 26, T5 3.03 us and 7.17 us).

Where

PR comment 5902523541, worker-leaves paragraph; ~/workspace/intel-AMX/exec/attnstats-20260929-w1/cpu-cell.sh:23-24 (the read loop); h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:926-933 (one read callback per leaf); README.stats.md:264-265 ('an interval is the difference of two reads')

Evidence
File times in leaves-latest (local, -0700): decode_like_forwards 16:39:01.205 (forwards 142), decode_like_totals .345 (attn_jobs 110,960 = 154.1 forwards x 720), decode_like_worker_26 .421, decode_like_worker_7 .441. Glob order (the shell's alphabetical expansion of the wildcard) reads worker_26 before worker_7. The 20 ms between them = 53 jobs = 1.47 forwards at 12.5 ms per forward, as observed. About 4 ms per FUSE read.
One attn job per worker per layer per forward: note_attn_job at self_attention.hpp:855 is called once per run_attention_job, one job per (operation, minibatch) in llama.hpp:751-770. 183,600 = 255 x 36 x 20.
Evidence strength

confirmed_by_data

Discriminating check

Read decode_like_forwards, then the worker leaves, then decode_like_forwards again during a run. The two forwards values bracket the skew.

Suggested fix

In the comment: 'rows read one file at a time over about 240 ms, between decode forwards 142 and about 162'. In README.stats.md after line 265, add one sentence. It should say that each leaf is one read, and that leaves read in sequence are therefore not one instant. The same sentence should tell a reader who needs per-forward numbers to bracket them with two reads of <class>_forwards.

Verifier notes

Merges lens findings CLAIMS-4 and PLAUS-1 (same defect, both confirmed by both refuters). The refuters' glob-order hypothesis is now confirmed by the poller script and file timestamps. The README path is the repo root README.stats.md.

TD-3 'stored K and V only' hides that kv_only jobs run attention as queries in every layer nit doc mismatchh/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:289-290; README.stats.md:255-256; PR comment 5902523541 last bullet
Trigger

A reader takes kv_only_jobs (comment, field comment, README) as jobs that did no attention work.

Consequence

The 8,128 KV-only prefill jobs are attention queries whose visits, K tokens and busy cycles are inside the prompt_or_mixed totals (path sets: none 0, avx 1,024, avx+amx 7,168 = 8,192). The primary definition 'token jobs without a listener' is correct in all three places. The wording 'stored K and V only' follows tron's own view of a token job, which must save K and V (do_kv) or have a listener [model.hpp:1809-1815]. So it is a wording choice, not a wrong counter.

Where

h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:289-290; README.stats.md:255-256; PR comment 5902523541 last bullet

Evidence
construct_minibatches batches every token job [model.hpp:1826-1830]. Only the K/V save tests token_jobs_do_kv [model.hpp:3008]. Listeners feed partition_listeners and setup_logits only [model.hpp:1713, :1725]. Path bits are set per visit with no listener check [self_attention.hpp:1650-1652].
Evidence strength

confirmed by code

Discriminating check

If KV-only jobs did no attention, the prefill token_jobs_by_path_set 'none' would read 8,128. It reads 0.

Suggested fix

Half-sentence in both docs and the comment: 'kv_only_jobs: token jobs without a listener (prompt tokens that produce no logits, the per-word output scores). They still run attention as queries in every layer. Their K and V are stored for later tokens.'

Verifier notes

Lens CLAIMS-1 rated should_fix. One refuter kept should_fix. The other downgraded to nit for two reasons: README.stats.md:257 defines token_jobs as all token jobs, and the path-set line in the same leaf contradicts the misreading. The '128x wrong per-query figure' consequence is not supported. Nit adopted.

TD-4 Header comment of attn_stats.hpp still says 'per attention worker' while rows are keyed by pool worker nit doc mismatchh/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:3-4 (vs :74 and :219-223); README.stats.md:237
Trigger

A reader opens attn_stats.hpp and reads lines 1-4, then sees seven attention-worker rows reading zero.

Consequence

Lines 3-4 say 'per layer and per attention worker'. Line 74 and lines 219-223 of the same file and README.stats.md:237 say rows and <class>_worker_<W> are keyed by pool worker index. bff317e0d3 changed the README and the Note but not the head comment. The posted data show pool workers 0 to 6 reading zero.

Where

h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:3-4 (vs :74 and :219-223); README.stats.md:237

Evidence
sed -n 1,6p: '// on each path (AVX software loop, AMX kernel, FPGA), per layer and per / // attention worker'. sed -n 74p: 'W in <class>_worker_<W> is the pool worker index.' README.stats.md:237: 'per layer and per pool worker'.
Evidence strength

confirmed by code

Discriminating check

grep -n 'per attention worker\|pool worker' h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp

Suggested fix

Change lines 3-4 to 'per layer and per pool worker'. One refuter reports the same stale phrase in the PR body Short version (scout-context.txt:287, not re-checked here).

Verifier notes

Both refuters confirmed. Two-word fix.

PR body items that are stale at the head

  • T1 definition: body says 'around state.forward(...) in the scheduler' [scout-context.txt:302]. HEAD takes T1 inside model::state::forward via forward_scope [attn_stats.hpp:30-33, model.hpp:1668]. Corrected in comment 5902534762, not in the body.
  • T5 definition: body describes the hardware join wait loop only [scout-context.txt:306]. HEAD measures both join modes, including the software-only failed-poll stretches [attn_stats.hpp:38-44, self_attention.hpp:1274-1284]. Corrected in comment 5902534762.
  • Test count: body says 't/t_attn_stats.cpp, 12 cases' [scout-context.txt:313]. grep -c TEST_CASE at HEAD = 19. Corrected in comment 5902534762.
  • Sample block [scout-context.txt:374-384]: labels without the rule suffix, a forwards leaf without listener_jobs / kv_only_jobs / n_attn_workers_min / n_attn_workers_max, join_wait_cycles 0, and TSC cycle counts of the 2026-09-24 run (busy 95,498,357,004, wall 8,518,609,356) instead of the 2026-09-29 run (busy 95,886,519,154, wall 8,610,367,272). The body discloses the label difference (:371), and comment 5902483127 says the body keeps the first-commit samples.
  • Verification list [scout-context.txt:390] ends at d85edc0e28. 8 later commits (1430736b2a, 6e23101744, 37bb2a4055, b6fd226982, ae8a6d38d9, 15f355acb2, bff317e0d3, 04da001cb5) have no build/test line. The posted runtron run covers bff317e0d3, and 04da001cb5 has unit-test coverage only (t/t_attn_stats.cpp:369-378).
  • Body sentence 'The final commit adds active_workers' [scout-context.txt:386] is still true (attn_stats.hpp:708, :729-733). But 'final commit' now means 04da001cb5, and the forwards record also carries n_attn_workers_min/max (attn_stats.hpp:467-474). One refuter's claim that the active_workers key no longer exists is wrong.
  • Short version reportedly still says 'per attention worker' [scout-context.txt:287, reported by a refuter, not re-checked here]. HEAD keys rows by pool worker (attn_stats.hpp:74, :219-223).

Counter and timer audit

Short version. Every exported counter and timer of PR 4596 at HEAD 04da001cb5 was traced from its writer to its FUSE key. Fourteen of the nineteen rows count what they claim with no reachable omission or double count. The open items are test gaps (the forward-level counters, the path-set histogram, the run_attention_job timer hooks and the FPGA hooks run in no test with the switch on), one design gap (T3 has no sample count), and documentation mismatches.

Words used here. / Counter = an atomic uint64 in attn_stats.hpp read by a FUSE leaf. / Visit = one apply_page_tok call (one query slice against one KV page for one KV head). / k_tokens = K tokens scored per KV head. / Cycles = TSC ticks (tsc_hz in the summary leaf converts them to seconds). / Row = worker_layer_row keyed by (forward class, pool worker, layer) [attn_stats.hpp:392]. / Class = decode_like or prompt_or_mixed, chosen once per forward [model.hpp:1668-1672]. / Pool worker of attention worker i = n_workers (the number of threads in the pool) - n_attn_workers + i [attn_stats.hpp:431-435].

Common facts for every row. Initialization: model_stats constructor allocates rows, fpga, timers and path_bits only when enabled [attn_stats.hpp:376-380]. Every counter starts at 0 except n_attn_workers_min, which starts at UINT64_MAX [attn_stats.hpp:300]. / Reset: none. Counters accumulate over the life of the model state and are never reset [attn_stats.hpp:71]. An interval is the difference of two reads. Per-forward scratch only is reset: fpga_bits reassigned [attn_stats.hpp:455], path_bits cleared after the fold (the end_forward step that combines the per-worker path bits of each token job) [attn_stats.hpp:570], T4 state reset [attn_stats.hpp:459-460]. / Destruction prints the stderr report [attn_stats.hpp:388-390]. / Aggregation: sum_layer sums all pool-worker rows of one layer and adds the layer timers [attn_stats.hpp:651-658]. sum_worker sums the layers of one worker [attn_stats.hpp:660-665]. render_totals sums the layers [attn_stats.hpp:739-756]. put_counts prints the keys [attn_stats.hpp:672-695]. put_fpga prints the FPGA keys [attn_stats.hpp:699-704]. / Every file:line below was checked with sed -n on the read-only worktree (a git checkout directory) at HEAD 04da001cb5.

{ready,pending}_{empty,avx,amx}_visits (per class; totals, layer and worker leaves)Yes. Unit: visits (apply_page_tok calls). The served path is set at exactly three return sites: amx for a dense 64-token page (a page whose 64 tokens form one range) [self_attention.hpp:1898], empty when no token of the page is scored [self_attention.hpp:1950], avx otherwise [self_attention.hpp:1982]. Per visit: visit_tally::record into a stack tally [self_attention.hpp:1650-1651 -> attn_stats.hpp:208-216]. Per apply_page_range call: add_visits(stats_worker, layer, tally, pass) [self_attention.hpp:1680-1685 -> attn_stats.hpp:481-485 -> worker_layer_row::add attn_stats.hpp:239-245], relaxed load+store (C++ memory_order_relaxed: no ordering with other memory operations) [attn_stats.hpp:176-179].Row of (class, pool worker, layer) [attn_stats.hpp:392]. {ready,pending}_{empty,avx,amx}_k_tokensYes for avx and amx. Unit: K tokens per KV head. amx adds page_size = 64 per visit [self_attention.hpp:1898]. avx adds relevant_k_tokens, the tokens actually scored [self_attention.hpp:1982]. empty adds 0 by construction [self_attention.hpp:1950]. So ready_empty_k_tokens and pending_empty_k_tokens are constant zero keys (R1-M24). {ready,pending}_avx_full_page_visitsYes: AVX visits that scored all 64 tokens of a page [attn_stats.hpp:212-214]. Unit: visits. With the kill switch (amx_qp null) a dense page takes the AVX path with 64 tokens. This key is therefore the kill-switch counterpart of amx visits (verified by the lens, the review agent that read the code for this table, at self_attention.hpp:1546, 1639, 1898, 1982). Zero at construction, never reset.None reachable. A full page written in the current prefill chunk (the prompt tokens processed in this forward) is not dense (one range per token). Zero at construction, never reset. One operation per forward; accumulates across forwards.None reachable: forward() is noexcept (declared in C++ as never throwing an exception). So there is no half record on unwinding (stack cleanup while an exception propagates). The comment claiming the destructor runs on unwinding is inaccurate but harmless (R1-M19, nit). Non-serializing (the instruction may execute out of order with its neighbours) rdtsc jitter of tens of TSC cycles is negligible at this scale (R1-M31, info). A grep of .github, ci, flake.nix and t/CMakeLists.txt for TRON_ATTN_STATS returned nothing. / Status meanings: / - checked_by_reading = a lens read the named lines and reached a conclusion. A test or run may exist in addition, and it is then named in the evidence. / - test_run_needed = only a build or a machine run can settle the item. / - not_checked = no lens read the relevant code. / Host limits: the unit tests of the head were built and run on this host (claude-box, an AMD Zen 5 machine) with TRON_AMX_DISPATCH=ON. All 12 runs passed (buildtest.json). This host has no AMX unit (Intel matrix instruction set) and no FPGA (programmable hardware accelerator). So the AMX kernel path and the FPGA path ran only through the test fakes (fake = a stand-in object that replaces the hardware in a test). The only machine runs of real AMX code are the author's runs on delphi-3bda (the Intel Xeon CI machine that has an AMX unit, CI = continuous integration) (the qwen-3-4b model, tp2 = tensor parallel over 2 accelerator cards, 8 users, prompt 1024 tokens, 256 generated tokens, USE_HW_ATTN=0 = CPU attention). The runs are reported in PR comments 5902483127 and 5902523541. testdata-result.json found those runs arithmetically consistent with the code. They were taken from binary bff317e0d3, 33 minutes before the head commit. No FPGA-assisted run exists in this review. / Words used here: / - forward = one model execution over the scheduled token jobs. / - token job = one token of one user's request scheduled into a forward. / - listener = the object that receives that job's logits (per-word scores). / - decode_like = every token job has a listener. prompt_or_mixed = at least one job lacks one. / - T1..T5 = the five timers, in TSC cycles (the CPU time-stamp counter): T1 forward wall time, T2 attention job time, T3 attention worker 0 job time, T4 period between operations on attention worker 0, T5 join wait. / - pool worker = a thread of the application thread pool, indexed by its pool position. attention workers = the last n_attn_workers pool workers. / - visit = one apply_page_tok call (one query slice against one KV page, a 64-token block of stored keys and values). / - path = which kernel served the visit (empty, avx, amx, fpga). / - FUSE leaf = a file under /model/<id>/attention/ served by the in-process FUSE file system (a user-space file system). / - kill switch = the environment variable TRON_AMX_DISABLE, which makes AMX report unavailable. / - TRON_AMX_DISPATCH = the option of CMake (the build configuration tool) that compiles the AMX kernels (default OFF, CMakeLists.txt:48). / - minibatch = one q_batch, one of the slices a forward's token jobs are split into (q_batch is the code's name for such a slice). / - chunked prefill = a long prompt processed over several forwards, one chunk per forward. / - EAGLE = a small draft model that proposes tokens for the target model. / - MoE = mixture-of-experts models (gpt-oss, mixtral, qwen-3-30b-a3b).

Finding ids (R1-Mn = merged finding n of the first review round, R2-Mn = merged finding n of the second review round) refer to findings-compact.json.

Test review

Each row of the test table names one test case or section that the PR adds or touches. It names the production boundary the case calls (a stats hook alone, or a production function such as apply_page_range or run_joins). It says whether a fake (a test substitute for real hardware or a real scheduler) keeps the information the assertion needs. It names the semantic break that would fail the case, and the breaks it does not catch. It gives a verdict. Of the 31 rows: / 25 are meaningful. / 4 are weak (they assert an implementation detail, restate the fix with the fix's own helper, or compare a value with itself). / 2 cannot fail in CI (continuous integration, the automatic test runs on GitHub). These rows only keep an existing section runnable when TRON_ATTN_STATS, the environment switch for the stats, is set to 1. / 0 carry a flaky-risk verdict. Flaky means a test that fails on some runs with no code change. Two rows note a low fork or TSC-read risk. The table did not raise that risk to a verdict.

Binaries and files (t/CMakeLists.txt at HEAD 04da001cb5). FILES, LIBRARIES, DEFINES, MODEL_REFERENCES, LABELS and ENVIRONMENT are the arguments of add_catch_test, the CMake helper that registers one test binary (LABELS and ENVIRONMENT are passed under PROPERTIES). / t_llama_unit = FILES t_llama_unit.cpp t_attn_stats.cpp, LIBRARIES tron pos_fake, DEFINES _GLIBCXX_ASSERTIONS (the libstdc++ macro that turns on bounds and precondition checks, applied to BOTH translation units, that is, to both compiled .cpp files), MODEL_REFERENCES llama-3.1-8b-instruct-good-tp2 + eagle-llama-3.1-8b-instruct-tp2 (tp2 = tensor-parallel over 2 devices), LABELS fake [t/CMakeLists.txt:596-603]. / t_heterogeneous_scheduler = FILES heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp, LIBRARIES tron fake_scheduler pos_fake, LABELS fake, ENVIRONMENT USE_HW_ATTN=1 (the environment variable that selects hardware attention, 1 = on) [t/CMakeLists.txt:580-583]. / t_amx_dispatch_dtype = default FILES t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp, LIBRARIES tron pos_fake, LABELS fake [t/CMakeLists.txt:605-607]. It is its own TU (translation unit, one separately compiled .cpp file). The reason is that it defines the amx_attn_h128g4 fakes. / t_compute_attention_unit = LIBRARIES pos_fake, LABELS fake [t/CMakeLists.txt:279]. / t/assertion_signal.hpp is a header shared by t_llama_unit, t_attn_stats and t_compute_attention_unit (a fork-and-waitpid helper that expects SIGABRT, the abort signal) [t/assertion_signal.hpp:15-27]. / Test selection: 'fake'-labelled binaries run in the host lane (the CI job that runs tests without an FPGA). The reason is that the host filter of bin/slice (the CI test-selection script) excludes only the 'fpga' label [bin/slice:1398]. / AMX compile option: TRON_AMX_DISPATCH (the CMake option that compiles the AMX kernel) defaults to OFF [CMakeLists.txt:48]. Only the legacy 'CMake and CTest' lane (CTest = the CMake test runner) passes -DTRON_AMX_DISPATCH=ON [.github/workflows/cmake-single-platform.yml:353-355]. That workflow has no automatic trigger. It runs only through workflow_call (a call from another workflow) or by hand [README.ci.md:9, README.ci.md:99]. / The default GCP Nix lane (the CI job on Google Cloud, built with the Nix package manager) configures without the flag. Its configure line has -DAVX512=ON -DENABLE_TESTS=ON and no TRON_AMX_DISPATCH [flake.nix:1200-1219]. So in default PR CI, t_amx_dispatch_dtype compiles only the SUCCEED stub (a test body that only records a pass) 'AMX dispatch dtype test skipped (dispatch not compiled)' [t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:262-266]. In that build, apply_page_tok never returns visit_path::amx. Proposed tests

  1. 1. One real forward with the stats on. Entry point: model::state::forward (the forward_scope at h/tron/models/model.hpp:1668-1672, note_attn_workers at :2303), reached through scheduler::forward() (h/tron/scheduler/full.hpp:1968). Fixture: t/t_trace_passes_host.cpp. Its three extend_one_token calls at :108-113 each run one real one-token forward on the llama-3-8b-host model (t/CMakeLists.txt:223-226). The test carries the CTest label fake (CTest is the CMake test runner). That label links the fake-device objects, a software stand-in for the FPGA card, into the test binary [t/CMakeLists.txt:151-152]. The test runs in the default lane (a lane is one CI build configuration, CI = continuous integration). Before the first extend_one_token, replace state.self_attn_state.stats with an enabled model_stats. The handle is scheduler_t = std::shared_ptr<tron::scheduler> (h/libtron.hpp:29). state is a member of the derived full_scheduler (full.hpp:1750). So the fixture needs one std::dynamic_pointer_cast to the full_scheduler of that model, or a small accessor. The swap is safe with FUSE unmounted or mounted. register_fuse_files keeps only a weak_ptr (h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:926). Alternative host: t/t_generate_mock_plugin.cpp:40-41 (tron::generate over the mock plugin, t/CMakeLists.txt:390-394). Assertions after the third forward: forwards[decode_like].forwards + forwards[prompt_or_mixed].forwards == 3 (the same count metrics.forward_passes reaches at :120). For each class, listener_jobs + kv_only_jobs == token_jobs. token_jobs summed over both classes == 3 (one token job per forward, not one token per forward). That check fails if :1672 passed in_tokens.size(). If sequence_prompt registers a listener per job, forwards[decode_like].forwards == 3. That check fails if the class condition at :1669 is inverted. n_attn_workers_max >= 1 and n_attn_workers_min <= n_attn_workers_max. Those checks fail if :2303 is deleted. sum_layer(cls, 0).attn_jobs >= 1. That check proves the run_attention_job hooks at self_attention.hpp:855 run through a real forward. stats->n_workers == attn.size(). About 15 lines.
