Verified correct

An item is "verified correct" when at least one review lens (one reading pass with a fixed question) read the cited lines of the read-only worktree at HEAD 04da001cb5 and found that the code, comment or document statement holds. The file:line references are the lens's reading evidence, not the output of a test run. The only executed evidence is buildtest.json. The four unit-test binaries of the head were built and run on claude-box (an AMD host with no AMX unit and no FPGA) with TRON_ATTN_STATS unset, set to 1 and set to yes. All 12 runs (4 binaries x 3 settings) passed. So an item that names a test case is confirmed to have run and passed there. Items about the AMX kernel path, the FPGA path and the runtron numbers rest on reading only.

Forward bookkeeping

  • Exactly one production entry point exists. state.forward is called only at full.hpp:2090, and run_forward is called only from forward(). So every forward passes through the forward_scope object (the per-forward record holder) [h/tron/scheduler/full.hpp:2090; h/tron/models/model.hpp:1697; grep of h/, src/, t/].
  • forward_scope is the first statement after the perfetto slice (perfetto is the tracing library, and a slice is one traced time interval) and before the latch guard (with_latch_arena, the object that marks the forward's latch arena, its pool of synchronization latches, as in use and clears it at the end). So T1 (the forward wall timer) starts after the slice opens. Its destructor runs after with_latch_arena's destructor and before the slice closes. This is what the T1 span of the Note (Note [Attention path stats], the explanatory comment block at the top of attn_stats.hpp) and the single-caller comment describe [model.hpp:1655-1673, 1658-1667, 1706-1711; h/tron/threading.hpp:262-266; h/tron/models/attn_stats.hpp:30-33, 852-857].
  • T1 covers logits (per-word scores) and listener delivery. run_logits waits on logits_done per chunk and on listeners_notified before returning. q_batches.clear() (q_batches is the forward's list of minibatches, the slices of token jobs that the plugin runs as a unit) and the arena release both precede the scope destructor [model.hpp:1774, 3317, 3320].
  • The class (decode_like or prompt_or_mixed) is decided once from in_listener_index.size() == in_token_jobs.size() and stored in current_class. Every per-forward hook reads current_class_ix(). So token_jobs, listener_jobs, kv_only_jobs, fpga_queries, token_jobs_by_path_set, visits, T2-T5 and n_attn_workers min/max all use that forward's class [model.hpp:1668-1672; attn_stats.hpp:453, 471, 484, 492, 508, 517, 526, 534, 542, 565, 593].
  • forwards and token_jobs move under one object. Both writers sit in ~forward_scope. The raw-hook half record (a record where only one of forwards and token_jobs advanced, reachable only by calling the hooks directly instead of through forward_scope) is documented and tested as a non-production path [attn_stats.hpp:879-883; t/t_attn_stats.cpp:406-430].
  • The forward_scope destructor is noexcept, and forward() is noexcept. So no exception path leaves a half record [attn_stats.hpp:879-883; model.hpp:1653].
  • The fold (end_forward's merge of per-worker path bits into per-job path sets) reads only the rows that the attention workers of the current split wrote (the split is forward_n_attn_workers, the number of trailing pool workers that act as attention workers). note_attn_workers runs once per forward and sets forward_n_attn_workers. end_forward folds rows [n_workers - split, n_workers) and clears them. begin_forward resets the split to UNKNOWN_SPLIT, so a forward without attention folds every row [model.hpp:2303; h/tron/plugins/llama.hpp:596; ingest/src/TronCpp.hs:1214; attn_stats.hpp:460, 560-571; t/t_attn_stats.cpp:767-806].
  • Empty forwards are excluded consistently. full.hpp returns before state.forward and before metrics.forward_passes++. So forwards and forward_passes agree, and forward_scope never receives zero jobs from the scheduler [full.hpp:1999-2005, 2101].
  • end_forward with zero token jobs and empty fpga_bits passes both asserts. With UNKNOWN_SPLIT the fold reads path_bits[w][job] only in vectors that begin_forward sized. So a plugin that never called note_attn_workers indexes nothing out of range [attn_stats.hpp:455-459, 556-559, 562-573].
  • With n_layers == 0 and the switch on, rows, fpga and timers are empty vectors, and no code path indexes them. row(), fpga_at() and timers_at() are reached only from hooks the mock plugin never calls [attn_stats.hpp:377-380].
  • The listener-count equality is exactly "every token job has a listener". One listener is registered per node (a node and an edge are the units of the scheduler's token tree), once per node per forward, always at a token that is a job. Both ensure_listener calls sit inside the frame that pushed the paired job. listener_index names the edge's last token. Edges have at least one token [full.hpp:1157, 2131-2139, 2137, 2364, 2375, 2455-2469; h/tron/scheduler/token_tree.hpp:480-481; model.hpp:1669].
  • TRON_ASSERT_LE(n_listeners, n_token_jobs) in begin_forward cannot fire through full_scheduler, including the EAGLE child scheduler. Every listener_index entry pairs with a distinct token job, and the vectors are rebuilt per forward [attn_stats.hpp:452; full.hpp:1941-1946, 2455-2469].
  • Partial-edge chunking never registers a listener without its job: the listener needs processed_tokens == token_count, and the descent stops at the children of a partially processed node [full.hpp:2455, 2933-2934].
  • A speculative verify step is decode_like for the target model, as the PR body and the forward_counters comment say. speculate.cpp sends one token per draft node with an exact-logits listener. full.cpp registers one listener per call [src/tron/scheduler/speculate.cpp:702-712; src/tron/scheduler/full.cpp:191-192; attn_stats.hpp:52-55, 294; model.hpp:1659-1662; scout-context.txt PRBODY:299].
  • The class comment's "1-token final prompt chunk" case is correct: such a chunk yields one KV job plus a listener attached to that same job [model.hpp:1661; full.hpp:2457-2459].
  • A node with a listener whose edge is entirely KV-ready yields a do_kv=0 query-only job with a listener. model.hpp accepts that job. So this forward is decode_like under the rule, consistent with the Note [full.hpp:2461-2468; model.hpp:1814-1815].
  • The EAGLE draft state is a full_scheduler child with its own state, model and model_stats (is_eagle comes from eagle_cfg.max_layers = 1 through the plugin). It enters through the same full.hpp:2090 site. It publishes under attention_eagle [full.hpp:1767-1777, 2090; src/tron/legacy_dispatch.cpp:73-77; llama.hpp:367; h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:496-499; attn_stats.hpp:919-920; t/t_attn_stats.cpp:903-926].
  • Every model::state constructs the stats object and its leaves. So no model's leaves are missing, and models that run no attention read zeros [self_attention.hpp:477-500].
  • assemble_attention_plan runs once per forward on the main thread before the workers are enqueued. So note_attn_workers and the note_fpga_query loop of construct_hw_plan run once per (forward, minibatch). A minibatch is one slice of the forward's token jobs that the plugin runs as a unit. Generated plugins (model plugins emitted by ingest, the repository's code generator in ingest/src/TronCpp.hs) assert one minibatch [TronCpp.hs:1184, 1211-1218, 1235-1238; model.hpp:1759, 2303, 2313, 2747-2755].
  • n_attn_workers_min renders 0 when no forward of the class ran, so the UINT64_MAX initial value never reaches a reader. Lens 5 cited attn_stats.hpp:816-819 for this render, but my grep at HEAD puts it at 774-777 [attn_stats.hpp:774-778; t/t_attn_stats.cpp:761-765].

Visit tallies and path bits

  • Path bits are written at the forward-level token job index tok_ix.i (items[batch_job_ix] is a token_job_id). That is the same index fpga_bits uses and the fold reads. The span is sized for token_jobs.size(). That size equals the forward_scope's count. forward() passes in_token_jobs.size() and then assigns token_jobs = in_token_jobs. token_path_span asserts that size [self_attention.hpp:1487-1489, 1603, 1647, 1652; model.hpp:1668-1672, 1681, 2752; attn_stats.hpp:439-443, 456-458, 566-569].
  • Path bits cannot leak between forwards. Each end_forward clears the bits it read for jobs [0, n_token_jobs). Rows beyond that range were cleared by the forward that wrote them. begin_forward only grows the vectors and reassigns fpga_bits per forward. end_forward asserts fpga_bits.size() == n_token_jobs [attn_stats.hpp:452, 455, 457, 556, 570].
  • No stale path bit survives a split change. A worker writes path_bits[pool w][job] only for jobs below n_token_jobs of the forward in which it is an attention worker. end_forward of that same forward clears exactly rows [first_row, n_workers) for those jobs [self_attention.hpp:1487-1489, 1652; attn_stats.hpp:568-571; t/t_attn_stats.cpp:767-806].
  • The pass index is right: run_sections passes its pending flag and apply_page_range maps it to PASS_PENDING_1 or PASS_READY_0 [self_attention.hpp:1171-1178, 1684].
  • Pending pages never count under a ready_ key: the page of every do_kv token job goes to pending_page_ixs, and run_sections passes the plan's pending flag to add_visits [model.hpp:1844-1848; self_attention.hpp:1171-1172, 1683-1684].
  • No visit is double counted across query slices. Every (query, page) pair appears in exactly one section. One apply_page_tok call is one visit. OR-ing the path bit gives the same result however many pages of one token set it [t/t_llama_unit.cpp:2660-2698; self_attention.hpp:1652].
  • Path values are exhaustive and exclusive: amx comes only from the dense AMX branch with 64 K tokens (one full page of 64 key tokens), empty only when relevant_k_tokens == 0, and avx otherwise with the scored count [self_attention.hpp:1889-1898, 1947-1950, 1982].
  • Kill-switch identity holds by construction. The dense predicate (begin 0, end 64, one visible range holding token 63, token 0 inside the window) is exactly the condition under which the dotter loop (the AVX loop that scores the K tokens of a page with dot products) scores all 64 tokens. So an AMX-on amx visit is a kill-switch avx_full_page visit. A full page that is not dense is avx_full_page in both arms (an arm is one run configuration, here AMX on or the kill switch). The AVX arm packs nothing. amx_on is false there [self_attention.hpp:1546, 1639, 1726-1728, 1922-1945].
  • On the kill switch and on AMX-less hosts, available() false makes amx_qp nullptr. apply_page_tok then reports visit_path::avx with the full page. avx_full_page_visits counts it, and the summary reports amx_available false [self_attention.hpp:1546, 1636-1644, 1898; attn_stats.hpp:213-214, 487; t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp kill-switch section].
  • Full pages written in the current prompt chunk take AVX even with AMX on. is_dense_amx_page tests the page's last token against the first token's range tok_hi, and a fresh page has one range per token [self_attention.hpp:1596-1597, 1727].
  • k_tokens is counted once per KV head. apply_page_range runs per kv_head, as the README says [self_attention.hpp:1466; README.stats.md:266].
  • A call that dismissed every page adds nothing: n_visits == 0 makes add_visits return at once, and the early return precedes any tally [attn_stats.hpp:483; self_attention.hpp:1508].
  • K tokens scored in software are equal in both arms. The kill switch only moves visits from amx to avx with the same relevant_k_tokens [self_attention.hpp:1898, 1982].

Timers

  • T2's window (run_attention_job's attn_elapsed) is unchanged by the PR. The reads keep attn_elapsed's value and its return. record_attention_time sees the same number. The upstream K/V wait stays outside T2. The diff only renamed the final stamp to t_end. The operation_ix overload's elapsed arithmetic around finish_attention_job is untouched [self_attention.hpp:820, 826, 832, 852-855, 860; llama.hpp:775; model.hpp:2138-2155].
  • The Note's T2 definition names run_attention_job's attn_elapsed explicitly and is exact for both plugins [attn_stats.hpp:33-35].
  • T3's t_entry is the first statement of run_attention_job on both plugins, and it precedes query_channel.start_reading. The generated worker's wait_attention_job and help_drain_gof sit before the call, like llama's begin_attention_job and rope_queries. So "entry to return" names the same interval in both plugins [self_attention.hpp:805-812; TronCpp.hs:1343-1360; llama.hpp:754-758].
  • T3 is gated on worker_ix == WORKER_0 and filed per layer in layer_timers, and T2 is keyed by pool_worker_ix, the same formula model_stats::pool_worker and run_joins use [self_attention.hpp:806-808, 855-858, 1342-1343; attn_stats.hpp:431-435, 515-520].
  • T4 closes once per operation, not per minibatch. The last operation of a forward has no period. begin_forward resets t4_last_operation on the main thread before the workers start. The period is filed under the previous layer with the current class. workers_done.wait() orders the previous forward's worker 0 before the next begin_forward [attn_stats.hpp:36-38, 459, 490-497; llama.hpp:710; t/t_attn_stats.cpp:240-255].
  • Generated plugins keep the T4 semantics: one minibatch and the ingest-known operation order [TronCpp.hs:1297-1307, 1317-1319].
  • T5 (the join wait) never double counts. join_hw is one bool per job. With it true, the poll is skipped. Then poll_waiting stays false, and only stream_hw_joins adds to join_wait_cycles. With it false, only the poll stretches (a stretch is one timed interval of waiting with no progress) add, as the comment says [self_attention.hpp:1152, 1256, 1267-1269, 1274, 1277-1283, 1332].
  • A run_joins poll stretch cannot be left open at loop exit. kv_heads_to_join.erase follows a passed poll, and that poll closed the stretch. The while loop exits only when the mask is empty [self_attention.hpp:1272-1284, 1339].
  • A stream_hw_joins wait stretch is always closed. The add follows the while(!any_ready) loop inside the same iteration of while(n_open > 0) [self_attention.hpp:1068-1153, 1152].
  • T5 lies inside T2 in both join modes. run_joins sits between the T2 reads. Both modes accumulate into the same join_wait_cycles, filed once per job. Stretches never overlap, so their sum stays below the job's wall time [self_attention.hpp:832, 842-843, 853, 1261-1265, 1274-1284, 1330-1332, 1341-1344].
  • tsc_hz in the summary is the calibrated TSC rate, started in system init and 0 only before calibration, as the header comment and README say [src/system/system.cpp:164, 1895-1899; attn_stats.hpp:724-726, 729-733; README.stats.md:265-266].
  • App-pool workers are pinned (each thread is bound to one CPU core). So the per-core TSC offsets that the calibration measures do not enter any same-thread timer delta [threading.hpp:821, 879-892; system.cpp:1871-1883].

FPGA path

  • note_fpga_pass is reached only when the pass is programmed. prepare_uniform_hw_attention returns when the plan is empty or the operation does not use hardware. The stats block sits inside the per-pass loop after tok_ix_arr is filled. So counts equal programmed work [self_attention.hpp:567, 575, 626, 629-637].
  • fpga_k_tokens per pass is the sum over the B queries of the pass (B = the number of queries in the pass) of (inclusive relative last index + 1). relative_hw_tok_ix returns an inclusive index on both paths. query_passes adds B [self_attention.hpp:600-620, 632-634; attn_stats.hpp:536].
  • fpga_queries is written inside the forward_scope lifetime. forward_scope is the first statement of forward(). run_forward reaches construct_hw_plan through the plugin's assemble_attention_plan. Every early return precedes the stats loop, so a rejected plan marks nothing [model.hpp:1668, 2313, 2518, 2519, 2736, 2747-2755; llama.hpp:596; TronCpp.hs:1214].
  • The fpga bit uses the same index as the join. note_fpga_query(q_batch->items[i].i) pairs with by_job[i], and by_job is positional over items. path_bits are indexed by tok_ix.i. end_forward ORs fpga_bits[job] with path_bits[w][job]. PATH_SET_NAMES indices 5, 6 and 7 match the bit values 4|1, 4|2 and 4|3 (the fpga bit 4 combined by bitwise OR with the software path values 1 = avx, 2 = amx and 3 = avx+amx) [model.hpp:2751-2752; h/tron/models/common.hpp:832-844; self_attention.hpp:1647; attn_stats.hpp:141-146, 567-569].
  • fpga_bits is sized to in_token_jobs.size() in begin_forward, asserted equal in end_forward, and note_fpga_query asserts token_job < fpga_bits.size() [attn_stats.hpp:455, 556, 591].
  • A token job served only by the FPGA gets the "fpga" set: empty visits add no software bit and the fpga bit comes from by_job [model.hpp:2747-2755; attn_stats.hpp:589-594].
  • T5 in the hardware join encloses exactly the no-progress wait loop. That loop ends on a ready pass or on sections_left == 0, as the Note and README say. The run_joins poll is skipped under join_hw, so nothing counts twice. T5 is filed under the pool worker of the attention worker with the same formula as note_attn_job [self_attention.hpp:790, 853, 1136-1152, 1140, 1274, 1342-1343; attn_stats.hpp:39-42; README.stats.md:285-288].
  • A worker with no jobs in its stride (its share of the jobs, selected by worker index) never enters the wait loop. A job with no FPGA passes in a hardware layer folds hardware trivially and waits for software only [self_attention.hpp:1056-1063, 1099-1108].
  • One prepare happens per (minibatch, layer): llama.hpp calls prepare_hw_attention for layer 0 and next_layer once per minibatch, and generated plugins prepare in the early phase or the late phase, never both [llama.hpp:643, 687; model.hpp:2091-2096, 2221-2227].
  • note_fpga_pass runs once per (forward, operation) on the main thread. The early phase runs inside barrier::next_phase's between_phases_action, executed by the caller. The late phase runs in prepare_late_hw_attention in the main stream (the sequence of generated statements that the forward's thread executes in order). The phase choice is a pure function of items.size() and a process-static value. Exactly one preparation statement per operation is enforced [threading.hpp:951-957; self_attention.hpp:744-752; TronCpp.hs:2285-2291, 2351-2358, 2362-2386; model.hpp:107-108, 2195-2198, 2219-2233; ingest/src/TronCheck.hs:625-642].
  • The header claim that FPGA counters are written on the main thread holds. plugin run() executes on the forward's thread, and the generated prepare calls are in the main statement stream. The writes are relaxed fetch_add (an atomic add with no ordering promise toward other memory) in any case [attn_stats.hpp:49, 535-536, 593; TronCpp.hs:2354, 2380, 2415-2422].
  • fpga_k_tokens_x_kv_heads equals fpga_k times facts.n_kv_heads, where facts.n_kv_heads is the model's n_kv_heads, the same constant construct_hw_plan and hardware_geometry use [attn_stats.hpp:693-698, 703; self_attention.hpp:208, 216-217, 489, 730-737; model.hpp:2557, 2680].
  • EAGLE draft states never take the FPGA path. hw_attn_eligible is never set for a draft state, and compute_operation_uses_hw is false. So there is no FPGA pass, no fpga bit and no hardware join [model.hpp:728-730, 1464-1468; self_attention.hpp:516].

Concurrency and lifetime

  • Rows have a single writer. apply_page_range keys by pool_worker(scratchpad, n_attn_workers) with scratchpad = worker_ix. note_attn_job uses the job's own pool_worker_ix. note_join_wait uses pool_worker(worker_ix, ...). So no two threads write one worker_layer_row inside a forward [self_attention.hpp:797, 855, 1176-1178, 1338-1341, 1484-1486, 1680-1685].
  • The main thread and the main helpers never write a row. The only row writers are apply_page_range, run_attention_job and run_joins on attention workers. T3/T4 go to the per-(class, layer) struct. FPGA and forward counters have no worker key [self_attention.hpp:855, 1176, 1342, 1683; attn_stats.hpp:530-543, 557-562].
  • layer_timers (T3, T4) have exactly one writer, attention worker 0. The T4 plain fields are written by worker 0 and reset by the main thread in begin_forward before the workers are enqueued. The pool queue and the workers_done latch give the happens-before order (the C++ memory-model guarantee that the earlier write is visible to the later read). The new worker 0 writes before it reads [self_attention.hpp:806-808, 856-858; attn_stats.hpp:353-362, 459-460, 491-493, 498-500; llama.hpp:607-611, 705-706; threading.hpp:140-144; h/system/mwaitx.hpp:190-203].
  • FPGA and per-forward counters use relaxed fetch_add, as the Note states. The plain per-forward scratch (fpga_bits, forward_n_listeners, forward_n_attn_workers) is written and read on the main thread only. 64-bit relaxed atomics with one writer per field cannot be read half-updated. So a FUSE reader sees old or new values only. The one documented inconsistency window (the three job counts) is stated in the header and README [attn_stats.hpp:47-49, 176-192, 450-461, 535-536, 543-544, 553-585, 577-579, 593, 604-624; model.hpp:1668, 2303, 2747-2755; README.stats.md:255-261].
  • forward_scope destruction runs after every pool task of the forward. The plugin's run joins its workers before returning. wait_for_coop_gof empties the tail queue. run_logits waits listeners_notified. The scope is declared before with_latch_arena. So its destructor runs last [llama.hpp:705-706; model.hpp:1668-1673, 1737-1750, 3147-3156, 3319; attn_stats.hpp:852-857].
  • No use-after-free is possible through a FUSE leaf callback. Each callback captures a weak_ptr and locks it for the render. fuse_sysfs copies the callback under a shared_lock and invokes it outside the lock in op_read, op_getattr and get_value. render_* take no lock. model_stats holds no pointer back to the state. After destruction, reads return "{}" [attn_stats.hpp:926-933; src/system/fuse_sysfs.cpp:628-634, 730-737, 939-950; t/t_attn_stats.cpp:632-634].
  • stats::unmount joins the FUSE thread (timeout then detach) before the handles are dropped. So the rinzler signal path does not destroy a state while a read is still running [fuse_sysfs.cpp:374-400; src/rinzler.cpp:4806].
  • Last-wins takeover (a later state with the same model id re-registers the leaves, and its callbacks replace the earlier ones) is safe. make_file returns the existing handle when the read_only flag matches, and all leaves pass LEAF_READ_ONLY_TRUE. The nullptr branch needs a read_only mismatch or a file-as-directory component (neither reachable for /model/<id>/attention*). So the TRON_ASSERT(handle) in stats::make_file cannot fire on a re-registration. set_read_callback replaces under a unique_lock [fuse_sysfs.cpp:149-158, 164-186, 987-996; attn_stats.hpp:156, 928; h/system/fuse_stats.hpp:113].
  • The takeover check is a map lookup that fires for a dead or an alive predecessor alike, and the README sentence describes both cases correctly [attn_stats.hpp:921; fuse_sysfs.cpp:286-295; README.stats.md:270-272].
  • The takeover info line fires only when the summary leaf already exists under the same prefix. In runtron that is the second and later schedulers of a --sequential or --isolated-iterations run [attn_stats.hpp:921-925; h/tron/stream_generate.hpp:1383-1394, 1453-1462].
  • Registration never races an unmount. rinzler builds its model states in the completions_controller constructor, after stats::start() and before app().run(), and unmounts only on the signal path. runtron unmounts in the unmount_guard at main exit. register_fuse_files checks is_initialized(). Post-start dynamic registration is what Note [FUSE Stats Startup Ordering] allows [src/rinzler.cpp:4554-4556, 4617, 4623, 4806; src/runtron.cpp:485, 511, 517; attn_stats.hpp:917; fuse_stats.hpp:133-169, 144-150, 160-163].
  • In runtron the FUSE tree outlives every state. unmount_guard is declared before eval and unmounts at main's return, after clear_model_cache and shutdown_app_pool. So no report prints after the unmount [runtron.cpp:485, 517-520, 525-526].
  • On rinzler's port-file failure path stats::unmount() precedes app().quit(). So the FUSE thread stops before model states are destroyed there [src/rinzler.cpp:4760].
  • runtron holds exactly one model state per scheduler, a local of stream_generate dropped at its return. The only other holders are weak_ptrs. So between prompt batches and after the last batch no state lives, and the leaves read {} [stream_generate.hpp:915; src/tron/token_cache.cpp:127-133; h/tron/token_cache.hpp:270; attn_stats.hpp:931-932].
  • runtron with a non-EAGLE --draft-model whose name differs from the target's aborts before any second state exists, and TRON_ASSERT aborts in every build [legacy_dispatch.cpp:56-57; stream_generate.hpp:920-921; h/common/assert.hpp:3-4, 69-74].
  • rinzler never destroys and rebuilds a state in-process. The controller registers only completions, chat/completions, models, health and info routes. No unload or reload path exists. Schedulers live in the controller map until ~completions_controller. POST /api/inference/down is a platformd endpoint (platformd is the host service manager that starts and stops rinzler), and it stops the whole process [rinzler.cpp:1937, 2337, 2457-2465, 2481-2492].
  • rinzler has no cross-process same-id collision. Every instance mounts its own FUSE tree [README.stats.md:171-181; rinzler.cpp:4397-4403].
  • Constructor ordering holds. cfg and model_ref are initialized before self_attn_state. So cfg.id and is_eagle read in the self_attention state constructor are valid. attn.resize precedes attn.size() [model.hpp:1048, 1049, 1084; self_attention.hpp:481, 495-497].
  • The only static is the process-wide switch env_enabled(), a function-local static const with thread-safe initialization. There is no thread_local. The constants at lines 337, 347 and 359 are constexpr [attn_stats.hpp:161-172, 337, 347, 359].
  • alignas(64) (64-byte alignment, one cache line) on worker_layer_row keeps neighbouring workers' rows on separate cache lines. path_bits is one vector per pool worker. So worker writes share no cache line except at vector boundaries [attn_stats.hpp:225, 343].
  • FUSE reads run on a single FUSE worker thread, and the host-side get_value() used by the tests also copies the callback under the shared lock [doc/fuse_stats_design.md:118; fuse_sysfs.cpp:940-947].
  • Callback output is not truncated on the read path. MAX_STRING_LENGTH applies only in op_write and set_value [fuse_sysfs.cpp:804, 904].
  • Mounted reads have no value cache: op_open sets direct_io=1 and keep_cache=0, and op_read re-invokes the callback on every call [fuse_sysfs.cpp:710-711, 731-737].
  • stats::unmount() followed by initialize() gives a fresh tree with no surviving handles (the node tree is owned by fuse_sysfs::root_). attn_stats keeps no handle. So the per-case test environments do not leak state between cases [fuse_stats.hpp:36-55; fuse_sysfs.cpp:104-118; attn_stats.hpp:928-934; t/t_attn_stats.cpp:92-113].
  • The in-repo merge-gate walk (a test in t/t_rinzler.cpp that lists the exported FUSE leaves and runs as a merge check) is rooted at <mount>/exported and treats only symlinks as leaves. So the attention/ directory and its JSON string leaves are never enumerated by it [t/t_rinzler.cpp:5238, 5262-5296].
  • expert_stats already registers unexported JSON-string leaves under /model/<id>/experts/router_N/. So the attention/ leaves add no new leaf category under /model/<id>/ [h/tron/models/expert_stats.hpp:176-193].
  • Neither the mock scheduler nor the proxy scheduler owns a model::state. So no model_stats is constructed there [src/tron/scheduler/mock.cpp:15-21, 67-70; src/tron/scheduler/proxy.cpp:20-26].

Worker and layer indexing

  • The worker mapping formula is identical at every launch and hook site. Attention worker i runs on pool worker n_main_helpers + i (n_main_helpers is the count of pool workers reserved as helpers of the main thread), with n_attn_workers = num_workers - n_main_helpers. That pool worker index equals num_workers - n_attn_workers + i. model_stats::pool_worker, run_attention_job's pool_worker_ix, estimate_worker_speeds and the generated worker's placement all use that formula. The formula matches Note [Attention workers are the last pool workers] [llama.hpp:595, 606-609; TronCpp.hs:1235-1238, 1255-1259; attn_stats.hpp:431-435; self_attention.hpp:797; model.hpp:2065-2066, 2255-2262].
  • run_sections asserts that the plan size equals n_attn_workers. So the n_attn_workers value reaching apply_page_range is the launch value [self_attention.hpp:1170].
  • There is no off-by-one at the split boundary. pool_worker asserts attn_ix < n_attn_workers and n_attn_workers <= n_workers. So its results lie in [n_workers - n_attn_workers, n_workers). end_forward's first_row uses the same lower bound for its fold loop [attn_stats.hpp:432-433, 562-564, 568].
  • Rows are sized by the pool, not the split. n_workers = attn.size() with attn.resize(app_pool().num_workers()). The pool size is fixed after creation. n_attn_workers is asserted <= num_workers. So pool_worker never indexes outside rows [self_attention.hpp:480-481, 496-498; threading.hpp:815-817, 855-863; model.hpp:2299; attn_stats.hpp:432-433, 469].
  • n_attn_workers_min and n_attn_workers_max are recorded per class once per forward from assemble_attention_plan_impl, after the asserts n_attn_workers >= 1 and <= num_workers, under the class that begin_forward set [model.hpp:1668-1672, 2298-2303; attn_stats.hpp:453, 467-474].
  • The fold cost on the main thread is n_token_jobs x forward_n_attn_workers byte loads and stores, plus one fetch_add per job. The fold touches every row only when the split is unknown. begin_forward loops over all pool workers once per forward to check sizes [attn_stats.hpp:456-458, 566-576].
  • The Note bullet the PR adds to [Attention workers are the last pool workers] is accurate: rows are keyed through model_stats::pool_worker and the fold takes the last n_attn_workers rows [model.hpp:2272-2275].
  • Operation ids are unique and dense on the generated-plugin path through the LoopyTron counter, the TronCheck schedule check and the compile-time checks (LoopyTron, TronCheck and TypedFxBulk are modules of the ingest code generator, under ingest/src/) [ingest/src/LoopyTron.hs:453-456; ingest/src/TronCheck.hs:591-598; model.hpp:1365-1375; h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:309].
  • model_layer.i == operation.i holds for every plugin at HEAD (TypedFxBulk counter for generated plugins, uniform_attention_binding for llama and mock). So a "two operations per layer" skew of T4 is unreachable [ingest/src/TypedFxBulk.hs:199-204, 909; ingest/src/LoopyTron.hs:1041, 1062; common.hpp:259-262].
  • For uniform-cache generated models model_layer.i < n_layers is enforced at compile time through the KV slot bound (slot = layer, n_slots = n_layers) [ingest/src/LoopyTron.hs:222-231; kv_cache.hpp:375, 742; TronCpp.hs:274].

Off-state cost

  • Per-visit hooks are guarded by one bool copied once per apply_page_range call. Line 1650 is the only per-visit test. The tally record and the byte |= run only inside it. apply_page_range holds no atomic and no rdtsc (the instruction that reads the TSC). The flush is one call per apply_page_range on the worker's own row [self_attention.hpp:1481-1482, 1650-1653, 1680-1685].
  • The off state computes neither the row key nor the path-bit span. So pool_worker() and token_path_span(), with their TRON_ASSERTs, are not called [self_attention.hpp:1484-1489; attn_stats.hpp:431-443].
  • No rdtsc runs with the switch off beyond the eight main already had. Lines 805-806 use UNSET_CYCLES_0. Lines 1137 and 1152 are pointer-gated. Lines 1277 and 1282 run only after stats_enabled at 1275 or poll_waiting at 1281, and poll_waiting stays false when off. Line 852 replaces main's final read with one read. forward_scope gates on on [self_attention.hpp:805-809, 852-854, 1136-1137, 1152, 1275-1283, 1332; attn_stats.hpp:876-882].
  • No worker hook uses an atomic read-modify-write or a shared write: inc_relaxed, max_relaxed and min_relaxed are a relaxed load plus a relaxed store, and the only fetch_add calls run on the main thread with the switch on [attn_stats.hpp:176-189, 535-536, 543-544, 575, 581-584, 593].
  • The hardware join accumulator is passed by pointer and is nullptr when off. So stream_hw_joins adds two pointer tests per wait-loop entry and nothing else [self_attention.hpp:1031, 1331-1332].
  • env_enabled reads TRON_ATTN_STATS once per process through a static local, accepts exactly "1", warns once for any other set value, and returns false when unset [attn_stats.hpp:161-172].
  • The stderr report prints only with the switch on and only when something was counted [attn_stats.hpp:388-390, 815-828].
  • No row, timer, FPGA counter or path-bit vector is allocated when off, and begin_forward and end_forward return at once [attn_stats.hpp:376-385, 451, 554; t/t_attn_stats.cpp:487-494].
  • page_tok_result keeps the size and triviality of the pair it replaced (static_assert). apply_page_tok keeps TRON(inline). So the three return sites add no call cost and no ABI cost (ABI: application binary interface, the calling convention of the compiled code) [self_attention.hpp:1823-1831, 1837, 1898, 1950, 1982].
  • The apply_page_range and apply_page_tok signatures are unchanged, and the new parameters sit on run_joins and stream_hw_joins, per job or per kv head [self_attention.hpp:1031, 1190, 1463-1475, 1839-1851].
  • forward_scope costs three bool tests and no write per forward when off, and it is constructed once per forward before with_latch_arena as its comment says [attn_stats.hpp:872-883; model.hpp:1658-1672].
  • The Note's claim "per visit ... no memory write and no atomic" matches the code [attn_stats.hpp:13-15; self_attention.hpp:1650].

Tests

  • The four test binaries of the head (t_page_share_counters, t_amx_dispatch_dtype, t_heterogeneous_scheduler, t_llama_unit with t_attn_stats.cpp) were built in the nix shell (nix is the package manager that provides the build environment) with TRON_AMX_DISPATCH=ON. Each binary ran three times, with TRON_ATTN_STATS unset, 1 and yes. All 12 runs passed on claude-box, a host with no AMX unit and no FPGA [buildtest.json rows; buildtest/summary.tsv].
  • All 19 test cases of t_attn_stats.cpp are listed by t_llama_unit --list-tests and ran. The binary reports 62 cases. The PR body's count of 55 cases was taken at d85edc0e28, before the round-4 tests [buildtest.json notes].
  • The 7 "ignored" warning lines under TRON_ATTN_STATS=yes are expected: the once-per-process static prints once per forked child of the descriptor-probe case plus once in the parent [attn_stats.hpp:161-172; t/assertion_signal.hpp; t/t_llama_unit.cpp:1458-1487; buildtest.json notes].
  • The two tests that drive the production apply_page_range hook assert both the tallies that must be nonzero and the tallies that must be zero. The fake lane (the t_llama_unit case that drives apply_page_range on a synthetic one-layer model; kv_mul 1, no AMX) expects 32 AVX pending visits of 32 K tokens each, and zero amx, empty or full-page visits. The dtype test (t_amx_dispatch_dtype, which drives the production apply_page_range for each CPU data type with fake AMX entry points) expects amx exactly when the kernels ran, else one full-page AVX visit, including the available()==false arm [t/t_llama_unit.cpp:2082-2083, 2205-2214; t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:183-198, 248-254].
  • The served-path value is covered by discriminating checks along the whole path: returning avx from the AMX branch fails the dtype test's dense expectation, and returning amx or avx from the empty branch fails t_llama_unit.cpp:2485 [self_attention.hpp:1898, 1950, 1828-1831; t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:186-189; t/t_llama_unit.cpp:2485].
  • W5 row keying is asserted through the production apply_page_range call. A 1-worker split puts the visit in the last pool row, with row 0 empty. The same attention worker 0 under a 2-worker split puts it one row earlier, the layer sum doubles, and n_attn_workers_min/max read 1 and 2. The pool_worker arithmetic, the rows across two splits and the worker leaves are asserted in t_attn_stats.cpp [t/t_llama_unit.cpp:2216-2233, 2243-2270, 2224-2230, 2262-2271; t/t_attn_stats.cpp:455-462, 666-683, 685-765].
  • W1 (software-join T5) is asserted at the production poll: positive and bounded with a late producer, zero when the sections are already done, and zero when the call's stats flag is off. The hardware wait loop is asserted positive in the mixed section and zero when every sweep makes progress. Both match the README's T5 sentences and the Note for both join modes [t/heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp:420-463, 471-556, 515-550; self_attention.hpp:1137, 1152, 1275-1283, 1341-1343; t/t_llama_unit.cpp:2911, 2965; README.stats.md:284-289; attn_stats.hpp:38-43].
  • The timing assertions are reliable, not intermittently failing. forward_scope's wall_cycles <= elapsed brackets the scope with same-thread rdtsc reads. The join sections take t_join_start before std::async and join_wall after worker.get(). So T5 <= join_wall holds by construction. join_wait_cycles > 0 is backed by 20 ms wait_for CHECKs that prove the worker was still blocked. The residual window is named in a comment [t/t_attn_stats.cpp:315-338; t/heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp:425, 427-429, 448-450, 454, 524, 536-538, 540].
  • The null-accumulator arm of stream_hw_joins executes in CI with the switch off. run_attention_job passes cached_operation_uses_hw, operation 1 uses hardware, and the Q-reuse (query reuse) section sets a hardware plan. So a missing null check would crash there (crash-detected, not asserted by value) [self_attention.hpp:1137, 1152, 1332; model.hpp:2123-2124; t/heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp:637-641].
  • W2 forward_scope is tested. The object records forwards, token_jobs, listener_jobs, kv_only_jobs, wall and the fold at destruction, and nothing before. It files the wall under its own class after a forward of the other class. It writes nothing when off. The three job counts move together [t/t_attn_stats.cpp:316-343, 337-339, 347-355, 357-366, 381-403; README.stats.md:256-261].
  • W3 listener_jobs and kv_only_jobs are asserted for 5/0, 1/4 and 0/5 splits (listener jobs / kv_only jobs out of 5 token jobs) and rendered in the forwards leaf [t/t_attn_stats.cpp:196-197, 214-225, 574-575; README.stats.md:246-252].
  • end_forward is tested to fold only the last n_attn_workers rows when the split is known and every row when it is unknown [t/t_attn_stats.cpp:767-806; attn_stats.hpp:560-571].
  • The FUSE contract of the README (summary on:false, totals and forwards {} when off, layer and worker leaves only when on, weak_ptr reads {} after the state dies, last-wins takeover, EAGLE prefix, unsafe ids skipped, rows/fpga/path_bits empty when off) is asserted. The Cursor-bot finding (Cursor bot is the automated code reviewer that comments on the PR) about the forwards leaf ignoring the off state is closed [README.stats.md:239-243; t/t_attn_stats.cpp:482-508, 503-506, 559-579, 586-664, 637, 646-648, 903-932].
  • The two-live-states-one-prefix behavior is asserted by a test (twin registered second dies first, leaves read {} while the first state is alive, re-registration restores it). The EAGLE prefix and the target prefix are both asserted [t/t_attn_stats.cpp:611-625, 903-916].
  • Eleven of the fourteen abort forks are discriminating for the TRON_ASSERT itself. With N_WORKERS_3 and N_LAYERS_4, the flat row index for worker 3 is 12 of 24, fpga_at and timers_at index 4 of 8, and pool_worker, note_attn_workers, token_path_span and end_forward compare without indexing. All thirteen aborting forks reach a TRON_ASSERT before any container index. The in-range control fork exits 0 [attn_stats.hpp:392-397, 407-411, 417-421, 431-435, 441, 469, 556, 580, 591; t/t_attn_stats.cpp:455-480].
  • assertion_signal resets SIGABRT to SIG_DFL in the child and _exits without destructors. TRON_ASSERT calls tron_abort then std::abort(). So SIGABRT is the right signal. The header replaces two identical copies with an unchanged body. Both old files keep <signal.h> and reach it through using namespace tron [t/assertion_signal.hpp:15-27; h/common/assert.hpp:59-73; t/t_llama_unit.cpp:73; t/t_compute_attention_unit.cpp:15; git diff 66c7bb8db1..HEAD -- t/t_llama_unit.cpp removed lines 33-44].
  • The fixtures satisfy the W4 asserts where they run with the stats on. The fake lane has n_layers 1 and one operation, and n_attn_workers 1 then 2, with the second guarded by attn.size() >= 2. check_page_range_reductions rebuilds the stats object when the pool grows to 4 and writes token_jobs once before the scope. exercise_page has n_layers 1, one job and split 1 [t/t_llama_unit.cpp:2069, 2081-2082, 2243, 2377-2378, 2382-2385, 2602-2621; t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:71, 111-113, 173-175].
  • No thread other than the test thread logs during the abort case (pool workers idle in latch waits, the TSC calibration loop has no log call after startup). So forked children cannot inherit a locked spdlog (the logging library) sink mutex [threading.hpp:772, 800; system.cpp:1925-1965].
  • stats_environment is self-contained per case. It unmounts on entry and in its destructor, with RAII scope (the destructor runs when the object leaves scope) inside the TEST_CASE body. Its only REQUIRE fires before initialize. unmount() is a no-op when nothing is initialized. The FUSE tree does not leak into the llama-state cases of the same binary [t/t_attn_stats.cpp:93-113, 95, 106-107, 589, 640, 906; fuse_stats.hpp:50-59; fuse_sysfs.cpp:121-123, 374-388].
  • register_fuse_files returns before any registration when the FUSE tree is not initialized. So t_llama_unit.cpp model states never register leaves. No leaf can hold a weak_ptr to the old object (grep for stats::initialize hits only t_attn_stats.cpp:104). So replacing state.self_attn_state.stats in those cases is safe [attn_stats.hpp:917-919; self_attention.hpp:496-497; t/t_llama_unit.cpp:2169-2175, 2185-2187, 2615-2617].
  • The stale t_llama_unit comment of the prior register (the item list of the earlier review round) is fixed at HEAD. The t_heterogeneous_scheduler comment is accurate: only t_attn_stats.cpp and t_tronstats_convention.cpp call fuse::stats::initialize [t/t_llama_unit.cpp:2172-2175; t/t_attn_stats.cpp:93-113; t/heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp:294-296].
  • Prior-register items A3/A4/A5/A6/A8/A9/A10/A12, A11, A14, A17/A18, A19, A22, A24, B6, B10 and B8 are closed at HEAD [t/t_attn_stats.cpp:288-290, 337-339, 432-480, 495-496, 498-499, 656-660, 861-890, 920-926; t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:248-254; t/t_llama_unit.cpp:2497-2498].
  • add_catch_test parses FILES as a multi-value keyword. So FILES t_llama_unit.cpp t_attn_stats.cpp builds both sources into one binary that CI collects. That satisfies the AGENTS.md rule that every committed test runs in CI [t/CMakeLists.txt:109-124, 596-597; config/test-benchmarks.json:147, 255, 327; AGENTS.md:22-28].
  • CI runs the whole t_llama_unit binary as one ctest (the CMake test runner) entry with --benchmark-samples=2 and at most a ~[tag] Catch2 (the C++ test framework) filter, with no splitting of the cases across separate runs [gen/t/CTestTestfile.cmake:151-152; t/CMakeLists.txt:210-214, 696-699; bin/slice:3159-3161, 3830-3832].
  • t_llama_unit.cpp precedes t_attn_stats.cpp in FILES. So the log_kv_footprint case that must run first stays first. The t_attn_stats.cpp cases construct no book (the KV cache storage object). So a reordering would not trigger the unisolated abort its comment warns about [t/t_llama_unit.cpp:283-286; t/CMakeLists.txt:597].
  • _GLIBCXX_ASSERTIONS applies to both translation units of the binary. So the attn_stats.hpp code compiled into t_attn_stats.cpp has libstdc++ bounds checks [t/CMakeLists.txt:192-194, 597-599].
  • The t_llama_unit fake-lane case matches the PR body bullet "32 AVX visits of 32 K tokens each": n_tokens = page_size (64), half = 32, visits == half and k_tokens == half x half [t/t_llama_unit.cpp:2082-2083].
  • The heterogeneous compile test covers the run_joins off arm with a late_producer_stats_off section expecting join_wait_cycles == 0 [t/heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp diff hunk "software-only join times its wait ... zero when the stats flag of the call is off"].
  • The existing assertion that every (query, page) pair appears in exactly one section backs the no-double-count claim for visits [t/t_llama_unit.cpp:2660-2698].

Docs

  • README.stats.md's off-state contract (summary reports "on":false, totals and forwards read {}) matches render_summary, render_totals and render_forwards. fmt (the C++ formatting library) renders bool as true/false. So "on", "eagle" and "amx_compiled" are valid JSON [README.stats.md:240-242; attn_stats.hpp:722-735, 739-757, 760-787; t/t_attn_stats.cpp:366, 503-506, 645-648].
  • README.stats.md's statement that per-layer and per-worker leaves appear only with the switch on matches the if (!stats->enabled) continue; before their registration [README.stats.md:243-244; attn_stats.hpp:936-944].
  • README.stats.md's "W in <class>_worker_<W> is the pool worker index" matches the row keys of visits, T2 and T5 and the pool_worker formula. One leaf is registered per pool worker 0..n_workers-1. Helper rows read zero, as README and header say [README.stats.md:273-278; attn_stats.hpp:74-75, 78-79, 410-414, 946-949; self_attention.hpp:797, 855, 1342-1343, 1484-1486].
  • README.stats.md's T3/T4 description (keyed per layer, observed by attention worker 0, absent from worker leaves) matches put_counts(with_layer_timers) and the WORKER_0 gates, and key presence and absence are asserted [README.stats.md:282-284; attn_stats.hpp:668-686, 745, 786, 797; self_attention.hpp:807-808, 856-857; t/t_attn_stats.cpp:566-572].
  • README.stats.md's T5 sentences for both join modes match the hardware wait loop and the software poll stretches, both filed into the same join_wait_cycles, and the Note's T5 sentence matches the README in meaning, not word for word [README.stats.md:284-291; self_attention.hpp:1135-1152, 1272-1283, 1341-1344; attn_stats.hpp:38-43].
  • README.stats.md's class rule and three job counts match the decode_like test in forward(), the begin_forward assert and the three consecutive relaxed adds in end_forward, and render_forwards emits listener_jobs and kv_only_jobs [README.stats.md:246-255; model.hpp:1668-1672; attn_stats.hpp:419-431, 547-555, 762-767].
  • README.stats.md's stderr class labels "decode_like/all_jobs_with_listener" and "prompt_or_mixed/some_jobs_without_listener" are the CLASS_LABELS used by print_report [README.stats.md:262-263; attn_stats.hpp:146-147, 834-837].
  • README.stats.md's EAGLE prefix and takeover info line match the attention_eagle prefix, the info line when a summary already exists, and the weak_ptr "{}" after the state is gone [README.stats.md:268-272; attn_stats.hpp:919-933].
  • README.stats.md makes three statements: attention workers are the last n_attn_workers pool workers, main-helper rows read zero, and the split range is recorded. All three match note_attn_workers, its call site and the Note bullet [README.stats.md:274-281; attn_stats.hpp:432-441; model.hpp:2272-2275, 2300-2303].
  • README.stats.md's fpga_k_tokens_x_kv_heads definition matches put_fpga [README.stats.md:266-268; attn_stats.hpp:693-698].
  • The Note's memory arithmetic matches the structs: worker_layer_row holds 17 atomics x 8 B = 136 B (48+48+16+8+8+8) rounded to 192 B by alignas(64), layer_timers holds 5 x 8 B = 40 B rounded to 64 B, and 27 workers x 36 layers x 2 classes x 192 B = 373,248 B as stated [attn_stats.hpp:83-85, 211-246, 250-263].
  • Note [Attention path stats]'s leaf list names exactly the five leaf families that register_fuse_files registers. No documented leaf is unregistered, and no registered leaf is undocumented [attn_stats.hpp:903-909, 934-944].
  • The PR body bullet that heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp follows the new run_joins and stream_hw_joins parameters matches the diff (run_joins(..., STATS_ENABLED_TRUE), stream_hw_joins(..., join_wait_cycles*)) plus the new software-only T5 section [pr4596.diff hunks for t/heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp].
  • PR-body figure: ready_amx_visits 10,278,144 = sum over t=1..255 of floor((1023+t)/64) x 36 layers x 8 users x 8 KV heads (2,304 = 36 x 8 x 8). Ready pages are full and visible. So is_dense_amx_page passes, and the AMX return counts page_size tokens. ready_amx_k_tokens 657,801,216 = 10,278,144 x 64 [self_attention.hpp:1726-1728, 1898; attn_stats.hpp:211].
  • PR-body figure: pending_amx_visits 6,912 = 3 x 2,304. The pending page has count 64 only at decode steps 64, 128 and 192 (end = page.count(), checked after the upstream K/V wait). pending_amx_k_tokens 442,368 = 6,912 x 64 [self_attention.hpp:826, 1873].
  • PR-body figure: pending_avx_visits 580,608 = 252 x 2,304 and pending_avx_k_tokens 18,579,456 = 4 (the four runs of partial-page counts 1..63: steps 1-63, 65-127, 129-191, 193-255) x (1+...+63) x 2,304 follow from the AVX return with relevant_k_tokens = tokens in the partial page [self_attention.hpp:1939, 1982].
  • PR-body figure: token_jobs_by_path_set for decode gives amx 24 = 3 steps x 8 users, avx+amx 2,016 = 252 x 8 and total 2,040 = 255 x 8, with path bits OR'd per visit and folded per job [self_attention.hpp:1652; attn_stats.hpp:567-575].
  • PR-body figure: attn_jobs 183,600 = 255 steps x 36 layers x 20 attention workers. note_attn_job runs once per run_attention_job per (minibatch, layer, attention worker), and ingested plugins (the model code that tron generates at ingest time) run one minibatch (one batch of token jobs processed in one pass) per forward. prefill attn_jobs 5,760 = 8 prefill forwards x 36 layers x 20 attention workers is consistent [self_attention.hpp:855; TronCpp.hs:263].
  • PR-body figure: period_count_w0 8,925 = 255 decode forwards x (36 - 1) layers. t4_last_operation is reset in begin_forward, and a period is filed only when the operation changes. So the last layer has no period [attn_stats.hpp:459, 490-497].
  • PR-body figure: prefill ready_amx_visits 16,515,072 = 128 queries per prefill forward x 56 (ready pages summed over the 8 prefill forwards of one user: 0 + 2 + ... + 14) x 2,304, and prefill ready K tokens 1,056,964,608 = 16,515,072 x 64 ("1.06 G" in the PR body) [self_attention.hpp:1726-1728, 1898].
  • PR-body figure: prefill pending_avx_visits 3,538,944 = 192 pending visits per 128-query prefill forward (the sees_page gate skips queries 0..63 on the forward's second page) x 8 prefill forwards x 2,304, pending_avx_full_page_visits 1,216,512 = 66 full-page pending visits per prefill forward x 8 prefill forwards x 2,304, and pending AVX K tokens 152,174,592 = 8,256 pending K tokens per prefill forward x 8 prefill forwards x 2,304 ("152 M" in the PR body) [self_attention.hpp:1605, 1617; attn_stats.hpp:213-214].
  • PR-body figure: K tokens scored in software are decode 676,823,040 = 657,801,216 + 442,368 + 18,579,456 and prefill 1,209,139,200 = 1,056,964,608 + 152,174,592, equal in both arms [self_attention.hpp:1898, 1982].
  • PR-body figure: "97.3 %" = (657,801,216 + 442,368) / 676,823,040 = 97.25 %. So the figure includes the pending-pass AMX K tokens, as the prose "went through the AMX kernel" allows. "658 M of 677 M" matches [PR body in scout-context.txt].
  • PR-body figure: "12.4 ms per step" = 8,518,609,356 TSC cycles / 255 steps / 2.7e9 Hz = 12.37 ms, with tsc_hz 2,699,999,710 Hz from the summary leaf [attn_stats.hpp:760-786].
  • PR-body figure: "20 attention workers were active" = 183,600 / 255 / 36 = 20 with n_workers 27 [PR body in scout-context.txt].

Main drift

  • There is no textual conflict between HEAD 04da001cb5 and origin/main 598b65f156. A dry-run three-way merge-tree (legacy git merge-tree, no objects written) has 0 conflict markers. The three files changed on both sides merge without manual edits [scratchpad mt.txt:1042, 1122, 1507].
  • main's rewrite of the query wait in run_attention_job (static_asserts on readiness_scope::whole_phase, removal of start_reading_at(0)) merges below the PR's T3/T4 entry stamp. The whole-phase wait is the same wait the fallback latch (one latch for the whole query channel, as opposed to a per-item readiness flag) did before. So T3 keeps its "entry to return" meaning [HEAD self_attention.hpp:800-813; origin/main self_attention.hpp:753-770 diff].
  • No caller of run_joins, stream_hw_joins, apply_page_range or model::state::forward was added on main, and every existing caller is updated by the PR [self_attention.hpp:842, 1330; t/heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp:433, 530; t/t_llama_unit.cpp:2907, 2960; full.hpp:2090; model.hpp:1668].
  • main's 161 commits since the base change model.hpp only in the expert-placement code (the code that decides which device holds each expert weight of a mixture-of-experts model: expert placement Note, model_is_tensor_parallel, expert_weights_are_single_tiles, ptensor.hpp include, gof_jobs_span, is_tiled_expert_weight, plan_uniform, spdlog (the logging library) text), not in forward(), run_forward(), assemble_attention_plan_impl or construct_hw_plan where the PR's hooks live [HEAD model.hpp:1656-1672, 1705-1710, 2297-2303, 2744-2755; git diff 66c7bb8db1..origin/main -- h/tron/models/model.hpp].
  • main's full.hpp change (indexed_vector gof_jobs, state.gof_jobs_span) has no counterpart in the PR. full.hpp left the PR in b6fd226982, when forward_scope moved into model::state::forward. So the W2 fix also removed that place where the two branches could diverge [git diff --stat 66c7bb8db1..HEAD lists no full.hpp].
  • The PR's added lines use none of the channel APIs main removed or renamed (is_fallback, producer_done, start_reading_at, finish_writing_at, channel_*_latch_backing, prepare(n_producers)), and t/t_attn_stats.cpp has no channel usage [grep count 0 over the PR's added lines].
  • The PR body's compile claim is correct. origin/main t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp passes 8 arguments to a 9-parameter apply_page_range (batch_queries has no default at HEAD or at base). The call is template-dependent and only instantiated under #ifdef TRON_AMX_DISPATCH. So the mismatch causes no compile error in a build without TRON_AMX_DISPATCH [origin/main t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:149-150; HEAD self_attention.hpp:1471; base 66c7bb8db1 self_attention.hpp:1382; HEAD t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:237].
  • PR #4557 (open, head c73e7fb2f9) fixes the same call with the same visible argument. So the two fixes agree [PR 4557 t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:157-159].
  • Perfetto (the tracing library tron uses for timeline slices) consumers read slice arguments by key name. So adding layer to Attention Ready/Pending and n_listeners to forward changes no existing query. main added no consumer of these slices [bin/analyze_kv_duration:19-23; git grep on origin/main perfetto/, .agents, doc].
  • attn_stats.hpp's includes (system/fuse_stats.hpp, system/fuse_sysfs.hpp, system/system.hpp, common/assert.hpp, common/util.hpp) are unchanged on main except a 2-line unrelated addition to h/system/system.hpp [HEAD attn_stats.hpp:99-106; git diff --stat 66c7bb8db1..origin/main -- h].
  • main refreshed four other rows of config/test-benchmarks.json. The t_llama_unit row is untouched on both sides, and the PR adds no file to that config. So there is no conflict [HEAD config/test-benchmarks.json:147, 255; git diff 66c7bb8db1..origin/main -- config/test-benchmarks.json].

C++ guide

  • forward_scope derives from the repo helper noncopyable<forward_scope> [attn_stats.hpp:867; h/common/util.hpp:54-60].
  • Every guard uses the h/common/assert.hpp macros (TRON_ASSERT_LT/LE/EQ), never <cassert>, as AGENTS.md line 73 requires [attn_stats.hpp:102, 393-395, 408-409, 432-433, 440-441, 452, 469, 556-558, 574, 580, 591; AGENTS.md:73].
  • Named constants carry the value suffix and uppercase style (N_PATHS_3, N_PASSES_2, PASS_READY_0, PASS_PENDING_1, N_CLASSES_2, PATH_BIT_NONE_0/AVX_1/AMX_2/FPGA_4, N_PATH_SETS_8, UNSET_CYCLES_0, WORKER_0, LEAF_READ_ONLY_TRUE, AMX_COMPILED_TRUE), and the test constants follow the same style [attn_stats.hpp:116-157; t/t_attn_stats.cpp:36-64].
  • Header includes match the symbols used (algorithm, atomic, cstdint, cstdio, cstdlib, cstring, iterator, memory, string, string_view, utility, vector, fmt and spdlog (the formatting and logging libraries), assert.hpp, util.hpp, fuse_stats.hpp, fuse_sysfs.hpp, system.hpp) [attn_stats.hpp:86-106]. self_attention.hpp adds <type_traits> and <utility> for the page_tok_result static_assert [self_attention.hpp:14-15, 1828-1831]. The test files already include <latch>, <future>, <chrono> and <optional> [t/heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp:2-12; t/t_llama_unit.cpp:9-21].
  • Note [Attention path stats] follows the repo's Note [..] plus tilde-underline convention and is cross-referenced by name from model.hpp and self_attention.hpp [attn_stats.hpp:6-7; model.hpp:1658; self_attention.hpp:471, 801, 1477].
  • One-line brace-less ifs conform to the guide's one-physical-line exception [attn_stats.hpp:164, 165, 389, 457, 877; self_attention.hpp:1152]. New multi-line bodies in production code are braced [self_attention.hpp:630-636, 1274-1284; model.hpp:2747-2755].
  • is_safe_model_id rejects empty ids, leading dots, slashes, quotes, backslashes and control characters (whitelist [A-Za-z0-9-_.:]) [attn_stats.hpp:889-899; t/t_attn_stats.cpp:656-660]. register_fuse_files returns before any make_file when is_safe_model_id fails [attn_stats.hpp:918; t/t_attn_stats.cpp:661-663, 928-931].
  • std::vector<worker_layer_row>(n) is sized once and never grows. So the non-movable atomic elements are safe: the vector never has to move them [attn_stats.hpp:377-379].
  • The model_stats constructor takes std::string by value and moves it into the const member [attn_stats.hpp:365, 371].
  • The TRON_AMX_DISPATCH ifdef (the build-time macro that compiles the AMX kernel in) guards the AMX facts. So the CI (continuous integration) lane that is built without AMX still compiles [self_attention.hpp:485-488].
  • The single-writer atomics scheme with relaxed memory order (the weakest C++ ordering: each access is atomic, but no ordering with other memory accesses is promised) is documented in the header [attn_stats.hpp:176-192]. It holds per row. add_visits, note_attn_job and note_join_wait are keyed by the pool worker that runs the job [self_attention.hpp:855, 1342-1343, 1484-1486]. Main-thread counters use relaxed fetch_add [attn_stats.hpp:535-536, 543-544, 575, 581-584, 593]. Readers load relaxed [attn_stats.hpp:604-624].

Open questions for the author

  1. R1-M1/R1-M2/R1-M8/R1-M9: will you add the four production-call-site assertions (forward class and token-job count through a real forward, path-bit histogram through apply_page_range, T2/T3/T4 rows through run_attention_job, note_fpga_pass through the fake-device fixture)? If not, state which of these you consider covered by the delphi-3bda (the Intel machine that runs the CI (continuous integration) tests) runs alone. State also why that is acceptable under the AGENTS.md test policy.
  2. R1-M27: should layer_timers (the per-layer timer record) carry a wall_count_w0 (the number of jobs summed) next to wall_cycles_w0 (the T3 sum on attention worker 0)? A reader could then compute the mean T3 per job when n_attn_workers changes between forwards (rinzler picks the split per forward), or when a forward has several minibatches (sub-batches of the forward's token jobs).
  3. R1-M13 and TD-1: will you re-measure the objdump (the binary disassembly tool) instruction counts and one A/A (a paired timing run whose two arms are expected to perform the same, used here to bound the cost of the switched-off code) confirmation round at HEAD 04da001cb5, or change the PR body and the 2026-09-30 comment to name the commits actually measured (cbf1bb6c0c and bff317e0d3)?
  4. R1-M15: do you agree to delete or shrink the case 'the hooks alone reproduce the half record' (t/t_attn_stats.cpp:406), which asserts the absence of a guarantee rather than a contract?
  5. R1-M7: is it intended that T4 (the layer period) closes on every operation change and is filed under the last attention layer? Under that rule a plugin (the code that defines one model architecture's forward) with two attention operations per layer, or with attention-free layers, would halve or stretch the period mean. Should an assert or a Note sentence make that explicit?
  6. R2-M13: in rinzler, two live model states with the same model id share one attention/ prefix. The first state's leaves are then no longer visible. Is that reachable in production (a repeated exec slug (the model id under which rinzler registers one --model-path entry), or a runtron self-draft (runtron -d naming the model as its own draft)), and is a per-state prefix wanted?
  7. R1-M75: should render_summary escape or sanitize model_id? Without that, the stderr '[attn-stats] model ...' line is not valid JSON for an unsafe id. The FUSE leaf, by contrast, is skipped silently for such an id (R1-M84).
  8. R1-M17: will the config/test-benchmarks.json rows for t_llama_unit, t_heterogeneous_scheduler and t_amx_dispatch_dtype be refreshed before merge, or does the PR body state why they stay?
  9. R1-M66: PR #4557 is open and touches the same t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp call site. Which PR merges first, and does the second one carry the manual merge note?

KEYS-5README attention section names only part of the key vocabularyThe README paragraph is an example under the casual-stats convention (the README rule for stats leaves outside /exported/: they need no description, unit, Prometheus type or manifest entry) [README.stats.md:225-235], and it delegates to attn_stats.hpp. Every key it names matches the code.

WORKER-1active_workers is a union over both forward classesThe code comment defines it exactly ('workers that ran at least one attention job'). README.stats.md does not mention the key. The posted run reports active_workers = 20/20 in both classes (20 active workers in decode_like and 20 in prompt_or_mixed). One refuter's claim that no active_workers key exists at HEAD is wrong (attn_stats.hpp:708, :729, :733 and the run summary print it).
WORKER-2The 20/20 split is a measurement, not a code guaranteeRefuted. The model is the ingested plugin (the model code that the ingest tool generates from the model file), and its max_minibatches = 1 [ingest/src/TronCpp.hs:263, model.hpp:640, config.hpp:103-109]. A minibatch is one group of token jobs that a forward processes together. That setting fixes the single-minibatch split at 27 x 2 / 7 = 7 helpers (27 pool workers, of which 7 become helpers and the other 20 attention workers) [model.hpp:1997-1999, :2014-2024] on every forward. The attn_jobs total of 5,760 jobs (= 8 prefill forwards x 36 layers x 20 attention workers x 1 minibatch) confirms one minibatch per prefill forward. The run flags were '--prompt-length 1024 -l 256 -u 8 --dont-stop' [exec/attnstats-20260924/campaign.sh:159], no --pay-for-determinism.

CLAIMS-3Prefill '152 M on AVX' (152 million K tokens scored on the AVX path during prefill) is not derivable from the posted samplesDerivable from the PR body total 1,209,139,200 plus the full-page rule verified on the decode sample (10,278,144 visits x 64 tokens per KV page = 657,801,216). It is recorded in the claims table. No code or doc is affected.
CLAIMS-5forward_scope unwind path has no testmodel::state::forward is noexcept (the C++ marker that forbids an exception from leaving the function) [model.hpp:1653]. So an exception terminates the process instead of unwinding. The destructor has one body [attn_stats.hpp:879-883], and t/t_attn_stats.cpp:304-367 already exercises it. At most an info nit that the comment at attn_stats.hpp:864-866 describes an impossible case.
CLAIMS-6Third comment announced but not postedIt exists: issuecomment 5902534762, posted 2026-09-30T01:55:25Z. Only the capture file pr-comments-testdata.txt omits it.
PLAUS-244 % of each attention worker's decode time is idle outside T2A restatement of the declared timer scope (T3 - T2 and T4 - T3 on worker 0 are documented at attn_stats.hpp:31-43 and README.stats.md:281-284). The decomposition is a passing consistency check, and it is stated in untimed_idle.
PLAUS-3Prefill T5 share 10 % vs decode 2 %: split estimator (the planner code that picks how many pool workers help the main work and how many run attention, from measured main and attention times) hypothesisThe planner lines (common.hpp:654, model.hpp:2495-2500, :2064-2066) are outside the PR diff. The hypothesis is untested by the posted data. A refuter's walk simulation (the refuter's own calculation of the wait share, not a measurement; est.) attributes about half of the share to page granularity alone. This is a planner follow-up, not a review item.

Method details

Tools: four runs of the Workflow tool (the script runner that starts and sequences Claude subagents). / Main review, first round: 2 mapping agents (test table, change map), 12 lenses (a lens is one review agent that reads the change from one fixed viewpoint), 1 merge planner (an agent that groups duplicate findings into merged findings), 3 refuters (agents that try to disprove a finding) per merged finding (87 first-round findings).

Main review, second round: 1 completeness critic (an agent that looks for areas the first round did not cover), 7 lenses, 1 merge planner, 3 refuters per finding (19 second-round findings). That run used 344 agents in all. The final synthesis agent (the agent that writes the report from the surviving findings) of that run could not emit the full report within its output limit. The report data was therefore assembled in code from the journal (the run's saved record of each step's output). The merge plan was applied. The refuter corrections were applied by majority. Six smaller agents wrote the triage, overview, counter audit, conditions table, proposed tests and verified-correct list. Two refuters checked the two tables that make new claims. / Test-data check: 4 lenses, 17 findings, 2 refuters each, 1 synthesis (39 agents). / Build and test: the head was built and its four test binaries were run on claude-box (the reviewer's build and test container, an AMD host without AMX) by the orchestrator (the workflow script that started the agents) (details under 'Tests actually run'). / Every line number was read at the head by the agent that cites it. The orchestrator did not re-verify every citation. The 'verifier notes' fields carry the refuters' warnings about the findings.

Rules applied to the prose: the plain-English rules of the reviewer's configuration (define terms at first use, one claim per sentence, units on numbers, citations after the sentence, a short version first, no idioms). A separate check pass over this page ran after generation. Its corrections are already applied.
